### T H E
# E D I T O R
### T O  T H E
# R E A D E R .

It is probable that in the following discourses <note>original: "discourses"; here meaning formal theological or philosophical treatises</note>, you may encounter some things which may be difficult to understand—not only because of the way they are expressed, but primarily because of their subject matter. My request, therefore, is that you will not judge and condemn what you may not be able to comprehend at first reading. Be careful that through rash criticism <note>original: "censures"</note>, you do not stumble upon the "hiding of power" <note>This refers to the idea that divine strength is often concealed from those who lack spiritual insight</note> which the prophet Habakkuk (speaking of Christ in the Spirit in chapter 3, verse 4) tells us is in his hand;

<summary>In this preface, the editor warns the reader that the upcoming theological treatises may be difficult to understand and urges them to avoid quick judgment, citing the biblical prophet Habakkuk regarding the hidden nature of divine power.</summary>
<keywords>preface, editor's introduction, Habakkuk 3:4, spiritual understanding, divine power, theology, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

Who is able to fully understand the specific progress and various stages <note>original: "gradations"</note> of the Holy Spirit in righteous minds? How can we perceive the various improvements, measures, and achievements—the different capacities and actions—that are unique to each soul as the "new man" <note>A biblical concept (Ephesians 4:24) referring to the spiritually reborn self</note> grows? It is impossible for us to set limits on spiritual things or to restrict that Spirit within ourselves or others. That Spirit is the fountain of divine light and life in all reborn <note>original: "regenerated"</note> souls, and it continually sends forth new streams and a fresh, unending flow of living water.

We are often too proud to understand God's graciousness <note>original: "condescensions"; in this context, it refers to God's willingness to descend to a human level to communicate with people</note> and the various ways divine truth and goodness are shared with us. Therefore, we cannot say that everything beyond our own understanding is merely imagination <note>original: "fancy"</note>, dreams, or delusion, nor can we claim that everything below what we have

Blessed be God, all reborn <note>original: "regenerated"</note> souls—even when they are furthest apart from one another here on earth—all meet in the divine understanding above! We feel too little of the love of Christ and are not sufficiently filled with that Spirit which is the source <note>original: "spring"</note>, the center, and the bond <note>original: "band"</note> for all good spirits in heaven and on earth.

Chapter 2, verses 12, 13, and 14, distinguishes these stages of growth into "little children," "young men," and "fathers." He mentions them twice together so that we might take more distinct notice of them. Similarly, the apostle Paul told the Hebrews that children have need of milk, but solid food <note>original: "strong meat"</note> belongs to those who are of full age (Hebrews 5:13, 14). 

I am certain that if the reader has truly come within the range <note>original: "compass"</note> of even the lowest of these stages of growth—if he has become even a "little child" in Christ—if he has even that genuine instinct and soulful desire toward God and divine things which is essential to and inseparable from the new birth in its most infant state, and if he has even the smallest degree of a spiritual mind, there are many things in this book which his soul must necessarily understand, respond to, and acknowledge as being exceedingly good. 

Therefore, let the goodness of what you *do* understand encourage your charity <note>In this context, "charity" refers to a kind and generous attitude toward others' ideas or difficult texts</note> to believe that what is currently beyond your reach may be just as good, or even better. reason not to judge and condemn it.

The Jews speak of this as one of God's first commandments to Moses, which he delivered to Joshua, and so down through all their elders and wise men in every age: to be slow in judging. It is certainly a much higher and more difficult task to be a judge in spiritual matters and things of another world—matters that are not obvious to the senses, which are superior to unenlightened reason, and above all the principles of the natural man <term>natural man</term> <note>natural man: In a biblical context (1 Corinthians 2:14), this refers to a person acting on human instinct and logic alone, without the aid of the Holy Spirit.</note>—than it is to judge the affairs of this lower world. Yet, courts of law <note>original: "judicature"</note> proceed with great wariness and caution toward judgment, even in small cases here below.

The apostle Paul cautions us in 1 Corinthians 4:5: "Judge nothing before the time, until the Lord comes." To pass a judgment or to pronounce a sentence upon people and principles in spiritual matters is, by its very nature, the greatest and most significant act our souls can perform; and therefore, it should not be hastily

<page-num>viii</page-num>
<header>THE EDITOR to the READER.</header>

executed; nor indeed should it be done at all without the presence of him from whom all the power, capacity, and authority of spiritual judgment is derived. God has committed all this judgment in a most special manner to Jesus Christ; and there can be no true and right judgment without him, or where he is not present.

Be persuaded then to do as God has done! Leave all judgment to the Son, according to the manner and measure of his appearing within you. Wait in stillness and silence for his coming—he who is to establish judgment on the earth and bring it forth within your spirit. Be able to say first, "I have full power by the Spirit of the Lord, and of judgment," before you take it upon yourself to judge your brother. Do not venture to step onto the throne of judgment <note>original: "judicature"</note> until he whose throne it is leads you up to it, lest you find yourself, before you realize it, in the seat of the mockers <note>original: "chair of the scorners"; a reference to Psalm 1:1, describing those who pridefully dismiss or ridicule spiritual truths</note> instead of the seat of judgment.

Judge nothing until Christ comes, so that you are not judged yourself and declared by him to be an evildoer and a meddler <note>original: "busy body"; in this context, it refers to someone who impertinently interferes in spiritual matters or the lives of others without authority</note>. It is true that the spiritual person <note>original: "spiritual man"; a reference to 1 Corinthians 2:15</note> is said to judge all things. However, the apostle, in the second chapter of his first letter to the Corinthians, clearly describes the only state of being capable of clear and distinct discernment; you must reach this state yourself before you can rightly judge your brother.

You may indeed speak ill of what you do not know, but you cannot judge it fairly until you truly understand it. Therefore, read the following discourses as a spiritual person, and in that spirit, give your judgment of them. Remember that every truth is not told to every good person all at once, nor is any truth revealed in its full glory at first sight.

"In my Father’s house," says Christ, "are many mansions." <note>original: "Chriſt, are many manſions"; a reference to John 14:2</note> This is true of his house of grace here below, as well as that of glory above. It corresponds to the progress we make through faith, love, and obedience.

Let every Christian then say of their present state, "This is my present mansion, my present heaven. Here it pleases God to receive me; this is how he reveals <note>original: "dispenses"</note> himself to me. Here I will give him thanks and will wait until I can, by the assistance of his grace, move higher." And if another person comes and speaks of things beyond my current comprehension and experience, I will not judge their light, nor any person’s liberty in my Lord. I am convinced of this: that just as Christ came in the flesh to his own and his own did not receive him, it may be the same when he comes in the Spirit.

<header>->The EDITOR to the READER.<-</header>

all sincere persons who differ from us; for we all stand in need of, and have a right to, such tender behavior from one another.

We may have wrong notions and opinions of spiritual truths even when we think we have the most correct <note>original: "rightest"</note> ones. Similarly, objects appear different depending on the quality of the medium <note>The substance or lens (like air or glass) through which something is viewed.</note> through which they are seen.

You confess yourself to be a poor, fallible creature; you bemoan your ignorance before the Lord and beg of Him that you may know yourself. Do not be too hasty, then, in charging your brother with errors while you yourself are not exempt from mistakes. Do not think yourself an infallible judge of his spiritual state while you are but a fallible discerner of truth for yourself.

It is impossible for us to have a perfect understanding of spiritual truths in this state of frailty. We now see only through a glass, darkly <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 13:12, meaning our current human perspective on divine truth is obscured and indirect, like looking through a clouded mirror.</note>. --- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the editor’s argument that human understanding is inherently limited and prone to error. He uses the metaphor of light and mist to explain why believers should be humble and avoid judging the spiritual "darkness" of others.</meta>

<page-num>xiv</page-num>

<header>->The EDITOR to the READER.<-</header>

better manage <note>original: "make a shift"</note> to puzzle and perplex the ideas of another person than know how to untangle and clarify our own. The sun does not appear to us in its true brightness and glory when we see it in the mornings and evenings through those earthly vapors and mists that come between us. Similar fogs are continually gathering between the God of truth and our understandings while we live here below. All our perceptions of spiritual truths are, at best, only like a twilight in which light and darkness meet. How unreasonable is it, then—while your own light is shaded with so much darkness—to think there is no mixture of light in your brother's darkness?

It is the incompleteness <note>original: "partiality"</note> of our knowledge which causes it to be dark. It turns into obscurity that which will be plain and pleasant to us when we finally see the whole body of divine truth—when we shall, in one view, behold the entire structure and uni- <note>The text likely continues as "universal" on the following page.</note>

<page-num>xv</page-num>

# The EDITOR to the READER.

...universal harmony of it; the connection and sympathy of one truth with another, and of each truth with every truth, which—being now seen in isolation—perplexes us. Yet we are all prone to run away with scattered bits and scraps of spiritual truth, with everyone calling their own small portion <term>pittance: a very small amount or portion</term> the entire whole. Some are zealous for one part of divine truth, and others for another part. While we contend for one, we for the most part let another go; and while we are seeking after one, we generally lose another.

We continually misunderstand one another, and the truth within each other, because of our partial perception of things. We have just enough light and knowledge—mixed as it is with darkness and ignorance—to allow us to dispute and quarrel with our brothers, but not enough to accept and comprehend them. Truth is unified in itself; but all truth is not yet reconciled in the mind of even the most perfect person on earth, although it is so in every...

<summary>The editor explains that human understanding of spiritual truth is often fragmented, leading to theological disputes and a lack of mutual understanding between believers.</summary>

<page-num>xvi</page-num>

# The EDITOR to the READER.

...sincere person's heart, who is in love with all truth even though he has not yet discovered its full scope and reach. I believe it is impossible in this life to achieve a complete view of the entire face of truth. Our minds and understandings are too limited <term>original: "contracted"</term> to take in those innumerable rays and beams of divine truth that are scattered and dispersed everywhere among all the children of light <note>A biblical term for those who follow God or seek spiritual enlightenment.</note>. You hold only one small part of it in all your ideas and opinions, while your brother holds another part in his. Instead of undervaluing his portion, it is much more appropriate for us to be modest and acknowledge the limitations of our own knowledge. We should say of God (as *Job* did on a similar occasion):

> “Look, these are just a part of his ways; but how small a portion is heard of him!”

*Job 26:14.*

The same spiritual truth may express itself to us through various different ideas and opinions. The entire state of things throughout...

What controversies do we see nowadays among even the best of people, concerning the most essential and vital truths of the gospel? For even they are not free from their own ignorance <note>original: "darkness"</note> and errors. What principle of Christianity has been universally accepted and explained in the same way by all good people, no matter how clearly and explicitly it is revealed to us in the literal text of the scriptures? 

Do we not see those very people who have (as we have reason to believe) accepted the same divine truths in their inner power, life, and spirit, yet interpret and preach them using concepts <note>original: "notions"</note>, opinions, and forms that are very different and even contradictory to one another? Do we not see them confusing <note>original: "perplexing"</note> each other with their own explanations of those very things on which they are fundamentally agreed in spirit and power? <header># The EDITOR to the READER.</header>

...to interpret each other's souls, we would quickly find that we differ more in words than in thoughts, and more in our mental concepts <note>original: "notions"</note> of things than in the things themselves.

Our spiritual wisdom and happiness do not lie in our ideas and opinions of spiritual truth, but in the truth itself. The apostle <note>referring to St. Paul</note> excellently distinguishes and explains this matter in 2 Corinthians 4:6–7. This spiritual truth—which is the shining forth of the glory of God in the person of Christ, the supreme universal truth—is the heavenly treasure. The ideas and forms in our understandings by which this truth appears to us are merely part of the earthen vessel which holds this treasure. In the same way, the same apostle <note>St. Paul</note>, speaking of this same subject, tells us, “The kingdom of God is not in words, but in power” (1 Corinthians 4:20). This kingdom does not consist of the excellence of our intellectual concepts <note>original: "notions"</note> and understandings, or of our words and expressions concerning spiritual things. Instead, it consists of the inner life, power, and spirit that is meant to change the whole person through the faith and love found in Jesus Christ, transforming them into a single image of glory. 

Spiritual knowledge is not merely a theoretical idea <note>original: "notional"</note>, but a real and practical reality. Divine truth is better understood as it opens and unfolds itself within the holy and heavenly mind and life of a sincere person, rather than through all the systems of theology <note>original: "divinity"</note> and "good books" in the world.

My soul, first ponder God’s immensity; as it is written, “Where <note>original: "Whither"</note> shall I go from your <note>original: "thy"</note> presence?” This globe of earth and sea is great, but compared to the deep space stretching from star to star, it is but a single point on a vast circumference. That vast deep is itself only a point compared to another space proportionally larger than our world is to a tiny ball. All of that, and twenty thousand more such deeps, is nothing compared with him. Even if they were all spoken into existence by him, it would leave him with nothing less, for he is still as infinite as he was before.

Again, meditate on his omnipresence; for no point or smallest center of

of the holy Second Principle <term>Second Principle: in mystical theology, this refers to the divine world of light, love, and grace, contrasted with the "first principle" of darkness or wrath</term>, which is a fifth form and quintessence <note>In alchemy and mystical thought, the "fifth form" or quintessence is the highest, purest state of matter or spirit</note>. What, short of that, can make you better than the devils? How can the most precise form of godliness benefit you any more than it does the one who can transform himself into an angel of light <note>A reference to 2 Corinthians 11:14, describing how Satan disguises himself</note>?

Then (O my soul), move forward toward meekness, integrity, faith, and love in holy fear until you come to the feet of your Jesus. Or, do not move at all, but remain a swine, a dog, a beast, and inwardly a devil; for if you go only part of the way, your fall—like a stone dropping from a high place—is most heavy. But to persevere

<meta>catchword: will</meta>

<summary>This page discusses the necessity of spiritual rebirth through the "Second Principle" and warns that intellectual knowledge of divine mysteries is useless without a genuine inner transformation.</summary>
<keywords>Second Principle, quintessence, Supreme Tincture, regeneration, mortification, first resurrection, spiritual transformation, angel of light</keywords>

<page-num>xxxiv</page-num>
### Meditations and Contemplations.

...will make you more blessedly happy than hell is miserably accursed.

Eternity is as incomprehensible as the eternal God, as far beyond men and angels as infinity is beyond our short, narrow, shallow, and superficial measures. This God, this Christ, this holy principle is our God, our Christ, our holy life, forever and ever; and if there were anything after "forever," it would be ours too.

Look then, O my soul, fixedly on this freehold <term>freehold: a permanent and absolute ownership of property; here used metaphorically for the soul's eternal inheritance in God</term>, and look disdainfully on all visible and invisible melting idols and airy bubbles. Breathe, press, pant, sweat, freeze, suffer, fight, run, watch, and persevere until your dry husk <note>The "dry husk" refers to the mortal, physical body or the outer self that must be shed to reveal the spiritual life</note> crumbles and falls off; then you shall reap, once and for all, more than the eye can see or the heart can conceive.

<summary>The author reflects on the incomprehensible nature of eternity and encourages the soul to focus on its eternal inheritance rather than the fleeting "bubbles" of the material world.</summary>
<keywords>eternity, Christ, spiritual perseverance, freehold, soul, transcendence</keywords>

<meta>This page presents a diagrammatic overview of the "Seven Spirits of God" or "Seven Fountain Spirits" as described by the mystic Jacob Behmen (Jakob Böhme). It illustrates how these spiritual qualities manifest differently across the realms of Hell, Heaven, the World, and the Earthly Kingdom, particularly following the fall of Lucifer.</meta>

### THE SEVEN SPIRITS OF GOD, or POWERS OF NATURE,

As they (since the Fall of Lucifer) show and manifest themselves in Love and Anger, both in the heavenly and hellish kingdom, and also in the kingdom of this world and the earthly kingdom. Taken from Jacob Behmen's *Mysterium Magnum* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; Latin for "The Great Mystery," Behmen's 1623 commentary on the Book of Genesis.</note>, Page 22.

---

| The Seven Spirits | Root Principle | Spiritual Manifestation | Natural/Material Manifestation |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1. **Astringent Desire** <note>This refers to the power of contraction, pulling inward to create density or "hardness."</note> | **Anger** | **Hellish:** Hardness, Cold | **World:** Cold, Hardness, Bone, Salt |
| 2. **Attraction** or **Compunction** of Sense <note>original: "Compunction"; here meaning a pricking, stinging, or restless movement.</note> | **Anger** | **Hellish:** Covetousness | **World:** Poison, Life, Growth |
| 3. **Anguish** or **Mind** | **Anger** | **Hellish:** Compunction, Envy | **World:** Senses |
| 4. **Fire** or **Spirit** | **Anger** | **Hellish:** Enmity, Pride, Anger | **World:** Sulphur, Perception <note>original: "Perceivance"</note>, Pain |
| 5. **Light** or **Love Desire** | **Love** | **Heavenly:** Love-Fire | **Earthly Kingdom:** Spirit, Reason, Desire |
| 6. **Sound** or **Understanding** | **Love** | **Heavenly:** Meekness, Divine Joy | **Earthly Kingdom:** Venus's Sport <note>Refers to the gentle, artistic, and generative principle of love and beauty.</note>, Life's Light, Speaking, Crying, Distinguishing |
| 7. **Body** or **Essence** | **Love** | **Heavenly:** Heaven | **Earthly Kingdom:** Body, Wood, Stone, Earth, Metal, Herb |

It contrasts how the same fundamental powers create hellish suffering or heavenly joy depending on whether they operate in "Anger" or "Love."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of Jacob Behmen’s (Jakob Böhme’s) mystical philosophy, moving from the Seven Spirits of God to the "Ten Forms of Fire." These forms correlate stages of spiritual and natural development with the biblical lineage from Shem to Abraham, illustrating the progression from the primordial will to the manifestation of holy flesh.</meta>

# The Ten Forms of FIRE. Extracted from Jacob Behmen's Answer to the first of the *Forty Questions of the Soul*. <note>original: "J. Behmen's Answer of the first of the forty Questions of the Soul"; referring to Böhme’s 1620 work *Forty Questions on the Soul*.</note>

---

**Form 1.** The eternal Liberty (possessing and being within itself the Will), signified by **Shem** <note>original: "Sem"; the eldest son of Noah.</note>.

**Form 2.** The state of being Desirous, signified by **Arphaxad**.

**Form 3.** The sharp drawing <note>Referring to the force of attraction or contraction.</note>, causing the opposing Will, signified by **Salah**.

**Form 4.** The Flash, or Lightning, caused by Liberty and resulting in Anguish, signified by **Eber**.

**Form 5.** Eternal Nature or the Great Mystery <note>original: "Great Mystery"; translates the Latin *Mysterium Magnum*, a central concept in Behmen's work representing the chaos or "abyss" from which all things are created.</note>, from which the two Kingdoms proceed, signified by **Peleg**.

**Form 6.** The two Principles of Fire and Light. **Reu** <note>original: "Regu".</note>.

**Form 7.** The Magic <note>original: "Magia"; Behmen uses this term to describe the creative, supernatural power of the will that brings spirit into substance.</note> making its own Looking-Glass: just as Life is composed of Fire and Water. **Serug**.

**Form 8.** The *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a technical term in Behmen's thought referring to the "disturber" or the spirit of wrath that breaks down or dissolves natural forms.</note> that breaks the outward Life, Strength, and Omnipotence. **Nahor**.

**Form 9.** The Virgin Tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; in alchemy and Behmen's thought, a spiritual essence or "color" that transforms and vivifies matter.</note>: Love-Fire: The Life of Angels and holy Souls. **Terah**.

**Form 10.** The Entrance into the holy Trinity <note>original: "Ternary"; refers to the divine manifestation or the "Threefold" nature of God.</note>: the giving of bodies <note>original: "corporising"</note> to Angels and holy Souls.

---

\* Which is the holy Earth, or holy Flesh, signified by **Abraham** <note>original: "Abram".</note>.

<summary>This page outlines Jacob Behmen's mystical "Ten Forms of Fire," mapping the spiritual process of creation and redemption onto the biblical genealogy from Shem to Abraham. It describes the transition from the "eternal liberty" of the divine will into the physical and spiritual manifestation of life.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the presentation of Jakob Böhme’s mystical system. Having previously outlined the "Ten Forms of Fire" and their biblical associations, the text now defines the "Three Principles"—the core framework Böhme uses to explain the relationship between God, the spiritual world, and the physical universe.</meta>

# THE THREE PRINCIPLES.

1.  **Principle 1:** The Source or Fountain of Darkness.
2.  **Principle 2:** The Virtue [or Power] of Light.
3.  **Principle 3:** The <term>Out-Birth</term> <note>original: "Out-Birth"; Böhme’s term for the physical, visible world which is "born out of" the internal spiritual worlds.</note> generated out of the Darkness by the Power of the Light.

## Represented by Analogy <note>original: "SIMILITUDE"</note>

1.  Man’s soul, giving rise to reason and thoughts, signifies the **Father**.
2.  The light showing the power of the soul and how to direct it represents the **Son**.
3.  The mind resulting from this light and governing the body resembles the **Holy Spirit** <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note>.

### ALSO

1.  The darkness in us which longs for the light is the **First Principle**.
2.  The virtue of the light by which we see intellectually is the **Second Principle**.
3.  The longing power proceeding from the mind, which attracts or <term>impregnates</term> <note>In this context, "impregnates" refers to the process of a spiritual idea taking on substance or form.</note> itself—from which the material body grows—is the **Third Principle**.

And note: there is a boundary <note>original: "Inclosure or Stop"</note> between each principle. See Chapter 7 of Jakob Böhme’s <note>original: "J. B."</note> book titled *The Three Principles of the Divine Essence*, verses 21, 22, and 23.

<summary>This page defines Jakob Böhme’s "Three Principles"—Darkness, Light, and the physical Out-Birth—using the Trinity and the human mind as analogies for these spiritual levels of reality.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Three Principles, Trinity, Out-Birth, spiritual darkness, intellectual light, mystical philosophy, human soul</keywords>

seven essential forms, or original working properties. # OF
# ETERNAL NATURE.

<image-desc>A horizontal divider composed of a single row of repeating typographic ornaments.</image-desc>

### What Eternal Nature Is.

<margin>1.</margin>
The subject concerns the essence of eternal nature. It is a most noble subject to explore. No one could lay a deeper foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> regarding this subject than the divine Jacob Behmen <note>Jacob Behmen refers to Jakob Böhme (1575–1624), a German Lutheran mystic whose influential but dense writings explored the relationship between God and the physical world.</note> has done; yet I find that he is understood by few and misunderstood by most. This has prompted me to search into the nature of this subject for my own private satisfaction, according to...

<summary>The author begins his treatise on the essence of eternal nature, identifying it as a noble but difficult subject previously explored by the mystic Jakob Böhme.</summary>

<keywords>eternal nature, essence, Jacob Behmen, Jakob Böhme, mysticism, spiritual philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's personal reflection on his spiritual journey and his method of inquiry, which relies on both his own intellect and divine revelation. He then transitions into a structured philosophical analysis of "eternal Nature" using classical categories of causation.</meta>

->[...continuing from the previous page]<-
the inward light of my own intellect and the inward discoveries of the Triune God <note>original: "Deity"</note> to the spirit of my soul. 

I shall confine my discourse to these two general heads:

First: What is eternal Nature in its original state and purity? 
Second: What kind of essence or principle is pure, eternal Nature?

I begin with the first of these.

2. Eternal Nature, as it came from the hand of Wisdom <note>Wisdom: Often referred to as Sophia in mystical traditions, representing the divine mirror or creative power of God.</note>, was a pure essence. For this reason, it is called "pure Nature." Being free from sin, evil, and every mixture of imperfection, it <note>original: "she"</note> was then entirely beautiful, clear, spotless, faultless, and sinless. Now, to reveal this great mystery of what eternal Nature is, I will explain it through its primary causes—that is, the efficient, material, formal, and final causes <note>The author employs the Aristotelian "Four Causes": the efficient (the agent that brings it about), the material (the substance of which it is made), the formal (the design or pattern), and the final (the ultimate purpose or goal).</note>.

# What eternal Nature is.

...above, before, and beyond the essence of eternal Nature? 4. We proceed to speak of the active agent of eternal Nature, which is God. We will briefly declare how God does not only create eternal Nature by way of his action <note>original: "efficiency"</note>, but also that he creates it out of and from himself by way of his own substance <note>original: "essentiality"</note>. Thus divine wisdom <note>original: "philosophy"</note> instructs us in Romans 11:36:
> "For of him, and from him, and to him, are all things."
That is, all things proceed from his own substance <note>original: "essentially"</note>; and if all things, then eternal Nature must proceed materially and substantially <note>original: "essentially"</note> out of God’s essence. As 1 Corinthians 8:6 says:
> "To us there is but one God, by whom are all things [as the active cause] and of whom are all things [as the essential substance]."
It cannot be otherwise, for when God was to create eternal Nature, there was nothing besides himself from which he might create it. Therefore, it follows that eternal Nature must proceed from God's own being <note>original: "essentially"</note> and substance. And indeed, God could not be

## Concerning the Eternal Element of Fire.

11. The eternal element of fire is the primary matter and foundation of eternal Nature's essence; therefore, we will speak of it first.

If we wish to understand the nature of this element, we must understand its origin and how God brought it forth. When it pleased the eternal will of God to move upon the surface of the abyssal <note>original: "abyssal"; referring to the infinite, bottomless depth of the primordial state before creation.</note> essence, He brought forth from it the four eternal elements to be the first principles of eternal Nature. He produced the element of fire first, along with its internal <note>original: "intrinsical"</note> and essential properties, to be the original beginning of eternal Nature's essence. Here you can see that its creator is the eternal will of the Triune Deity—Father, Son, and Spirit—for they share but one will among them.

--- Page 70 ---
<meta>This page continues the detailed description of the "eternal element of fire," specifically breaking down its internal qualities and its relationship to the "four forms of eternal nature" as described by Jacob Boehme.</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>
<header>Concerning the eternal Element of Fire.</header>

This element was created by God to be a fierce, mighty, penetrating, and consuming essence. This is evident from the essential and inseparable properties it was created with, all of which reveal the nature of this created fire-essence. 

The first of these properties is darkness, which consists of a tightening <note>original: "astringent"</note> harshness. From this dark harshness arises bitterness and its prickliness; out of this bitter prickliness arises eternal woe and tormenting anguish, called the "sting of bitter anguish." Finally, from this bitter sting, the fierce fire-essence is born. This is how Behmen <note>Jacob Boehme (1575–1624), the German mystic mentioned on the previous page.</note> describes it, and I agree with him; he identifies the fire-essence as the fourth form of eternal nature. That is to say: the dark, tightening harshness is the first form leading to the production of fire; the bitter sting is the second; anguish is the third; and the flash of fire coming out of the anguish is the fourth form, which completes this eternal fire-essence.

However, it may perhaps make the nature of this fire-essence clearer if I add the following properties: to the fire-essence belong fierceness, wrath, sternness, the qualities of sulfur <note>original: "sulphurousness"</note> and saltpeter <note>original: "salnitrousness"; saltpeter or potassium nitrate was associated with explosive energy and "nitrous" air in early chemistry.</note>, as well as consuming, devouring, and flying up and

**Question:** If you ask me, what does the goodness of the fire-essence consist of?

**Answer:** I answer: in all its essential properties, for they are all good and useful for the purpose for which they were created. By means of them, the eternal fire-essence becomes a fit instrument for God to work with. Therefore, there is no evil property to be found in this fire-essence; if there were, God would have to be the author of it, as he is the sole creator of fire. But indeed, God created it to be a good servant, to serve his eternal will, not—

### Concerning the Element of Eternal Water

15. We will now proceed to the element of eternal water.

This element is the next matter of eternal Nature’s essence, and consequently should be treated next after the fire. Its efficient cause <note>the primary agent or force that produces an effect</note> is the same as that of the fire-essence, namely, the eternal will of God. The center from which it is produced is the same: the eternal nothing, or abyssal essence <note>original: "abyssal essence"; likely referring to the *Ungrund*, the "bottomless" or "groundless" void from which the divine nature originates in theosophical thought</note>. The manner in which it is produced is also the same, namely, by divine power and wisdom. Therefore, we refer you to what has been said before concerning the eternal element of fire.

The nature of this eternal element of water is a meek, mild, soft, and gentle essence. For just as the fire-essence is the basis <note>original: "ground"</note> of fierceness, so the water is the basis of meekness. <header>Concerning the eternal Element of Earth.</header>

To understand what this element is, we may consider its cause <note>original: "efficient"</note>, the foundation from which it originated, the manner in which it was brought forth, and the purpose for which it was produced. These are the same as those mentioned regarding the element of fire.

The essential properties of this earth are weight <note>original: "ponderosity"</note>, physical form <note>original: "corporeity"</note>, and transparency. For this eternal earth is not like the external, physical earth—thick and opaque—but is instead a transparent, crystalline earth. Yet, it provides essence and physical form to the three previously mentioned elements. Therefore, it was created by God to make the essence of eternal Nature substantial. Fire, water, and air must have a single foundation or substance in which to exist and through which to move among one another; that substance is the element of earth.

This element, <term>Jacob Boehme</term> <note>original: "Behmen"</note> says, is the seventh property of nature. In it, all the other six properties move within a single foundation, just as the soul moves within the body; this is very well expressed by him.

This concludes our discussion concerning the matter of which eternal Nature consists: namely fire, water, air, and earth.

## Concerning the <term>Formal Cause</term> of Eternal Nature.
<note>In traditional philosophy, the "formal cause" refers to the design, pattern, or structure that gives something its specific identity and essence.</note>

18. The formal cause of eternal Nature’s essence is nothing more than the mixture and harmonious composition of the four eternal elements, for they exist within one another and flow through one another. Let us now consider who orchestrates the mixing of these eternal elements and their properties, for they would never mix on their own, even over an eternity, because of their opposing <note>original: "contrariety"</note> natures. Therefore, we must conclude that divine Wisdom is the artist who blends these elements together.

19. Regarding the mixture of these eternal elements, consider with me these following details:
First, the art of Wisdom is revealed in the way they are mixed; they are blended with one another and through one another, and their opposing natures cannot hinder or resist the skill and power of Wisdom.
Secondly, the art of Wisdom is shown not only in mixing them, but in bringing

The fierceness of the fire is mitigated and calmed <note>original: "allayed"</note> by the water; the harsh astringency <note>A sharp, contracting, or tightening quality.</note> of the darkness is dissolved in the gentleness <note>original: "meekness"</note> of the light, and so on for the rest.

The fierceness of the fire gives strength to the gentleness of the water, and the gentleness of the water calms the fierceness of the fire. Likewise, air is very useful to the fire to keep it from being suffocated; and the earth is useful to them all, because it gives them a body in which to act and move.

# The Formal Cause of Eternal Nature.

We can consider further the usefulness of the elements to one another, as they are harmonized and balanced <note>original: "tempered"</note> together by the hand of Wisdom. The fire gives life, mobility, and strength to the gentle <note>original: "meek"</note> water, and the water gives food and nourishment to the fire, thereby soothing <note>original: "allays"</note> the fire’s fierce hunger. Thus, darkness exists within the light, and the light within the darkness, satisfying the harsh, bitter hunger of the darkness by acting as food for it. 

The natural goodness of these elements consists in this mutual usefulness to one another; for how can any evil be in them, since they all serve the will of their Creator and are useful to each other? The darkness is as useful as the light, and the fire as the water; consequently, they are all good. Their opposing natures <note>original: "contrariety"</note> are harmonized and reconciled by the *skillful hand of Omnipotent Wisdom.*

Fifthly, Wisdom’s art appears in the fact that, in this balanced mixture <note>original: "temperature"</note> of the eternal elements, she makes them interact <note>original: "qualify": in this context, to affect or impart qualities to one another.</note> and serve one another in triumphant joy, and to rejoice in how they each affect the other. **Answer:** I define *eternal Nature* in this way: *eternal Nature* is an eternal essence, existing <note>original: "subsisting"</note> as a six-fold working property, where each part is inseparable from the others. For the *darkness* generates *harshness*, and the *harshness* generates *bitterness*. This bitterness leads to *anguish*, and the *anguish* leads to *fire*. This fire produces *water*, and the *water* produces *light*. This light generates the *love-fire*, and the *love-fire* generates *air*. In this way, they generate one another and interact <note>original: "qualify"; meaning the properties influence and blend with one another</note> within and through each other inseparably and without division. However, in the *seventh* property, they all find rest as if in a single *foundation* <note>original: "ground"</note>. 

So, *eternal Nature* consists of six working properties within one single *foundation* or substance. When all the properties of *eternal Nature* are considered in relation to this one *foundation*, they are all equally eternal; none of them can be ranked as existing before the others. They all share only one beginning and one mother, and they exist together in one *foundation*. 

In this union, they constitute the *wrestling wheel of eternal Nature* <note>The "wrestling wheel" is a central concept in the mysticism of Jakob Böhme, representing the dynamic, circular motion of spiritual forces as they struggle and balance one another</note>. In this wheel, sometimes one property is uppermost and sometimes another: sometimes *darkness*, sometimes *light*, sometimes *anguish*, and sometimes *joy*. 

Nevertheless, even though they are all undivided and none exists before the others, for the sake of making a distinction, we may place the *darkness* and *harshness* in the

# The formal Cause of eternal Nature.

is the true form or formal cause <note>In philosophy, the "formal cause" refers to the pattern, structure, or essence that makes a thing what it is.</note> of eternal Nature.

21. But I imagine I hear someone object to what has been said: that I seem to contradict the divine Behmen <note>original: "Behmen"; referring to Jakob Böhme (1575–1624), an influential German Christian mystic and theologian</note>, who places darkness and light back-to-back as opposites, and makes fire a distinct center from the light. Does he not speak of a "cross-birth" in eternal Nature which divides the fourth form (fire) from the fifth (light)?

To this I answer: what Behmen says is absolutely true, but it must be noted that *he speaks of eternal Nature in its fallen and degenerate state, whereas I speak of it in its original, spotless purity.* Behmen speaks of God within nature (about which I intend to write more later, in order to remove the veil of obscurity that seems to cover all his deep and mystical writings). *But I am now speaking of the birth of eternal Nature in the context of God introducing himself into the essence of Nature.* Therefore, I do not contradict Behmen’s writings at all. Based on some of his works, it clearly appears to me that his spirit had been lifted up into the outer court of the bottomless <note>original: "abyssal"</note> globe of eternity.

<summary>The author clarifies that while Jakob Böhme describes a conflict between light and dark in a "fallen" nature, this text describes Nature's original, pure state where these forces are harmonized. The author argues that their views are compatible but focus on different states of existence.</summary>

## Concerning the Final Cause of Eternal Nature.

22. Although it is true (generally speaking) that the final cause <term>final cause: In philosophy, the "final cause" is the ultimate purpose or goal for which something exists.</term> and end for which all creatures—and consequently eternal Nature—were brought forth, is the *good pleasure and will of God*, just as the Holy Scripture teaches in many places. For example, Ephesians 1:9 says, "According to the good pleasure of his will, which he purposed in himself." Again, in verse 11, it says he is the one "who works all things according to the counsel of his own will." Revelation 4:11 states, "And for your pleasure all things were created." Yet, where it pleases God to reveal the grounds and reasons for his creative will, we may then look into them and consider them.

23. The first purpose why eternal Nature was produced was for *manifestation*: so that the Triune Deity <note>The Holy Trinity: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.</note> might reveal themselves, and along with themselves, the "still eternity." This was the reason why the Trinity raised up within themselves an eternal will to produce eternal Nature—so that they would no longer be hidden in their abstract nature of pure spirit and naked Deity <note>original: "naked Deity"; referring to God's essence as it exists in itself, apart from any created or manifested form.</note>.

A second purpose <note>original: "end"; here meaning the goal or objective.</note> for the creation of eternal Nature was so that all the glorious attributes of God—his self-sufficiency, omnipotence, wisdom, goodness, and immensity—might be displayed and made known.

A third purpose was so that all the wonders of the Deity might be revealed and put into action <note>original: "brought into act"</note>.

Fourthly, eternal Nature was produced so that it might be the raw material <term>subject matter: In this context, the fundamental substance or "building blocks" used by God to form the universe.</term> out of which all creations and all worlds would be brought forth. Eternal Nature is the storehouse of the Holy Trinity, wherein a full supply of all the instruments and materials required for creation are stored. 

So, if eternal Nature—with its eternal elements, forms, and active essences—had not been brought forth by the Trinity, then no worlds or creatures would have ever existed. Nothing would exist except the "still eternity," <note>The state of God's existence in absolute stillness and unity, prior to any movement toward creation or manifestation.</note> which would have been contrary to the will that the Holy Trinity had formed within themselves to reveal themselves through creation. To fulfill that will, the essence of eternal Nature was produced.

### What kind of Principle eternal Nature is.

I shall next show you what kind of principle eternal *Nature* is.

**Question:** If it be asked what kind of essence eternal Nature is?

**Answer 1:** I answer first that it is a created essence, as has been sufficiently declared before.

**Answer 2:** Eternal Nature is an eternal essence, for *whatever is immediately <note>original: "immediately"; meaning directly or without any intervening cause or medium</note> created by God out of the divine chaos is eternal*, because it proceeds out of an eternal root. Now, since eternal Nature is immediately brought from the abyssal chaos <note>original: "abyssal chaos"; the "Abyss" or "Ungrund" in Behmenist philosophy, referring to the bottomless, unsearchable void or potentiality of God before manifestation</note>, it must consequently be eternal.

**Answer 3:** Eternal Nature is an original essence; that is, IT IS THE FIRST CREATED ESSENCE, out of whose fruitful womb all CREATED ESSENCES proceed. *It is the first essence of all essences, and the ground and source of them.* There is nothing above, before,

# What kind of principle eternal nature is.

The fact that created essences <note>original: "essences"; the fundamental nature or "being" of a thing</note> did not immediately proceed from God will appear through the following reasons:

First, if all creatures had proceeded immediately from God, they would all have to be a perfect unity, without duality, diversity, or conflict <note>original: "contrariety"</note>. This is because God’s essence, from which they would have proceeded, is a perfect unity. However, we find that created essences are not a perfect unity; instead, diversity and duality are found in them, which are not present in the divine essence. # What kind of Principle eternal Nature is.

**Answer 8.** Eternal Nature is a good essence. There is no evil in it, despite the fact that it is made up of opposing forces <note>original: "contrarieties"</note>. For God, who is eternal goodness, could not make that evil which he designed to be the original matter of all created essences. If he had, he would have passed evil down <note>original: "entailed"</note> to all his creatures who were to come from its womb. 

Nor was it partly good and partly evil; for this is a mixture that God declares against, and therefore he would never be the cause of it himself. We cannot say that evil exists within the essence of eternal Nature; rather, it is swallowed up by the good, because nothing can come from God except what is completely good. The darkness in eternal Nature is as good as the light, and the fire as good as the water. All the forms of eternal Nature must necessarily be equally good for the following reasons:

**First**, because they have one active cause <note>original: "efficient cause"; a philosophical term for the primary source or agent that brings something into being</note>, one Creator: God himself.

**Second**, because they all have only one mother: the abyssal chaos <note>original: "abyſſal chaos"; referring to the infinite, formless void or "abyss" from which creation emerges in mystical theology</note>, from whose womb they proceed.

**Question.** But you will ask further: if evil is not in God, nor in eternal Nature, where then will you place it? What will you make the ground and source of evil?

**Answer.** I answer with certainty, that
> "**THE MUTABILITY <note>original: "mutability" — the capacity to change or be inconsistent.</note> OF THE CREATURE IS THE CAUSE OF EVIL. For though all things were created good by God, yet they are not unchangeably so, but may become evil.**"

But we will speak no further of this matter, because this is not the proper place to speak of the origin of evil and sin; for we are here only treating of eternal Nature as it came out of God’s hand, and before any creatures were created or brought forth out of it.

**Objection.** You may object that Jacob Behmen <note>original: "Jacob Behmen" — referring to Jacob Boehme (1575–1624), a famous German Christian mystic whose complex descriptions of God and Nature influenced many later thinkers.</note> makes eternal Nature the ground of both good and evil.

**Solution.** To which I answer again, that what Jacob Behmen writes concerning eternal Nature is very true; for he does not make it the ground of evil in its state of purity, as it came out of God’s hand, but as it is in its fallen state. And the cause of

This mistake occurs because Boehme <note>original: "Behmen" — a common English spelling of the name of the German mystic Jacob Boehme (1575–1624).</note> is not well understood. Sometimes he speaks of eternal Nature in its state of original purity, and immediately after speaks of it in reference to its fallen state. Because this distinction is not clearly recognized by unsuspecting readers, they conclude that Jacob Boehme makes eternal Nature in its original purity the source <note>original: "the ground"</note> of evil; however, that is actually contrary to the entire purpose of his writings. 

Boehme was very aware that many would be likely to misunderstand his writings in this way, which led him to say that *there was a veil over his writings that would prevent all those who were not born again <note>original: "born again" — refers to a spiritual rebirth or inner transformation central to Christian mysticism.</note> from having a correct understanding of them.* 

Whenever Jacob Boehme attributes the source of evil to eternal Nature, he considers <note>OCR original: "confiders" — likely a transcription error for "considers."</note> it in its fallen state, as it became infected by the fall of Lucifer into the hellish principle, and in that regard, I fully agree with him. However, I am speaking here of eternal Nature in its pure, undefiled state, as it first came from the hands of God.

**Objection.** You may say that darkness is an evil essence; but how can that be evil which is the cause of the light?

This is the "feeling life" <note>a state of existence characterized by sensory and emotional awareness</note> of nature, and it proceeds from the love-fire's dominion in and over all its properties.

**Answer 10.** Eternal Nature’s essence is a blessed and happy essence. I do not mean that she is the *highest good* <note>original: "summum bonum"</note>, the most high and sovereign happiness—for that is only to be found in the *divine essence*—but that *she is as happy and blessed as a created essence is capable of being.* This happiness of eternal Nature consists in this: that she is free from all conflict <note>original: "contrariety"</note>, strife, misery, and anguish.

It could not be otherwise, unless we would make God the author of conflict, misery, pain, and death—which is directly contrary to his Nature and to what the holy scriptures testify concerning him. No, *eternal Nature, as she came out of the hand of her great Creator, was all beautiful, all spotless, and all happy.* No strife, misery, or death was to be found in any part <note>original: "borders"</note> of her dominions.

And here again, I desire it may be noted that I do not contradict *Jacob Boehme* <note>original: "Jacob Behmen," a 17th-century German mystic whose complex ideas about God and Nature heavily influenced this text</note>. <page-num>78</page-num>

### Concerning God in eternal Nature.

Spiritual theologians <note>original: "divines"</note> mention two great mysteries in the divine nature. The *first* is the Trinity in Unity, and Unity in Trinity, which concerns the Deity in their single and abstract essence. The *second mystery is* the Deity in humanity, and humanity in the Deity, which concerns God as being introduced and existing <note>original: "subsisting"</note> in eternal Nature. In the previous part of this discourse, I treated the first of these; I now intend to speak of the latter in this chapter by exploring this proposition: that *God has introduced himself into the principle of eternal Nature.*

This proposition is explained through the following details:

1. First, when I say that God has introduced himself into eternal Nature, I mean that the blessed Trinity—Father, Son, and Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note>—has introduced itself into eternal Nature. This includes the Father with his intellectual, all-seeing eye; the Son with his flaming heart of love; and the Holy Spirit with his outgoing, active power.

# God's Union with Eternal Nature

I shall explain and reveal <insert>to</insert> you the nature of this union in the following points <note>original: "particulars"</note>:

1. # God's Union with Eternal Nature

...perceive the effects of God's love and hatred, mercy and justice, sweetness and severity; yet in pure nature—into which *God has introduced himself, and which is our subject here*—nothing can ever be found except the effects of an *unchangeable love*. This unchangeable love is the bond <note>original: "band"</note> that creates the inseparable union between God and pure nature, between the Deity and humanity. 

This love passes through and penetrates every single part of *eternal Nature* in a *vital* <note>meaning: living or organic</note> way. In turn, *eternal Nature* presses into that love, which makes the union between them most *intimate*, *vital*, and inseparable. Thus, we may ask: who shall or who can separate God and pure nature? Or who can nullify <note>original: "disannul"</note> the eternal covenant between them? 

However, despite everything said about this union between God and the principle of pure nature, we must understand that this union is *without confusion*. This means *God is not pure nature, nor is pure nature God*. For though one exists within the other, nature cannot *comprehend* <note>original sense: to fully grasp or contain</note> the Deity; rather, God *comprehends* nature.

### Why God Introduced Himself into Eternal Nature

**Question:** At this point, one might ask: what were the purposes <note>original: "ends"</note> and motives for the blessed Trinity introducing themselves and entering into such an intimate union with the principle of pure nature?

**Answer:** I shall answer this inquiry with the following points:

**First reason:** The reason the Trinity brought themselves into the principle of pure nature was to manifest themselves in it and through it. Although they were already revealed to themselves as pure <note>original: "simplified"</note> spirits in the "still eternity" <note>A mystical state of divine rest or potentiality existing before the unfolding of the created universe</note>, they desired a further revelation of themselves to the creatures that lay hidden in the womb of eternal Nature. For this reason, they entered into union with it.

**Second reason:** A second motive or reason was so that the Holy Trinity might glorify and exalt their own divine nature of eternal love within those worlds and creatures that were to be brought forth from the womb of eternal Nature. In the still eternity, there were indeed an infinite number

# God’s Union with Eternal Nature

...of pure <note>original: "simplified"</note> spirits who, dwelling in the center of love, glorified and exalted the divine nature of love, which was all in all to them. However, God's purpose was to have his eternal love glorified and exalted in an infinite variety of creatures and beings. These beings, in their respective ranks and according to their individual capacities, could all exalt and glorify love, thus creating a divine symphony <note>original: "concert"</note> and harmony, which cannot exist without variety.

**The third reason** why the Holy Trinity introduced themselves into eternal Nature was to manifest their sovereignty and supremacy. Their sovereignty could not be demonstrated without a variety of dominions and subjects. Both of these were brought forth from the womb of eternal Nature by the Holy Trinity. The "dominions" were the various worlds created out of the principle of nature, and the "subjects" were the countless number of creatures inhabiting each of those worlds and principles. Through both of these, the sovereignty of the Holy Trinity was fully declared and revealed.

> "And though God is in fallen nature, yet it is not for the same purpose for which he was pleased to enter into <note>original: "introduce himself in"</note> pure nature. For he entered into pure nature so that he might be the sole mover and actor in it, and thereby keep and preserve it in its original purity. Whereas the reason why God entered into fallen nature was that he might redeem it, and restore it to its original purity, from which it has fallen."

**Question:** But you will ask, how can the supreme purity of the divine nature be preserved within the unclean vessel of defiled and corrupted nature?

To which I answer: "If we consider what has been said, we shall find that the **divine Nature is the highest unity and <term>simplicity</term>**, <note>In theology, "simplicity" refers to the concept that God is not composed of different parts but is a single, indivisible essence.</note> and consequently cannot admit of any mixture or composition with anything, being free from all things. Though he is in and penetrates through all things—like the sunbeams, which pierce the air and water, penetrating them throughout—yet they are not mixed or blended <note>original: "jumbled"</note> with them, but remain in their perfect liberty, untouched by either of them."

# God’s Union with Eternal Nature

*To conclude, you can see how God the Father has entered into the principle of pure nature as an eternal eye that looks through the entire globe of nature. Along with himself, he has brought his eternal, flaming heart of love, which acts as an eternal spring flowing continually through the principle of eternal nature for its delight and refreshment.*

*His Spirit of life serves as the supreme moving force throughout the entire essence of pure nature. In this way, the divine nature and glory of the Deity is hidden within nature, like a jewel in a cabinet or a treasure hidden in a field. This treasure is to be found only by those holy souls who—through great earnestness and constant labor—have achieved the presence of the Holy Trinity within themselves through their spiritual baptism into the nature of the Father, Son, and Spirit.*

*These souls have arrived at perfection by the redemption, purification, and <term>transmutation</term> <note>original: "transmutation"; in this mystical context, it refers to the spiritual transformation of a person's "impure" or fallen nature into a divine state, much like the alchemical process of changing base metals into gold.</note> of their impure nature, and have thus been restored to their original purity.*

<page-num>iv</page-num>

-># PREFACE.<-

The primary purpose of such men is to guide us to the Fountain of Wisdom, which is open at all times to all people and is ready to be given to everyone who truly seeks wisdom there. From this universal source, every person receives according to the strength of their desire and their capacity to understand.

Accordingly, it is required that everyone should use their gift according to the grace they have been given and the measure <note>original: "proportion"</note> of faith they have received. Now, faith is the personal evidence of each individual, and not that of another. Thus, although one person may have a larger measure of insight, or something more mysterious, there is no conflict <note>original: "disharmony"</note>; rather, everything contributes to the benefit of the whole and teaches wisdom, so that everyone may be presented as perfect in Christ. And although we cannot understand all truth, let us remain humble and sincere seekers, and in due time we shall be rewarded with her treasures.

# PREFACE.

->**PREFACE.**<-

In the considerations that follow regarding the scope of Jacob Behmen’s <note>original: "J. Behmen"</note> writings, the writer demonstrates the truth of the doctrines laid down by Jacob Behmen. He bases this on symbols <note>original: "types"</note>, figures, and sacrifices found in the Old Testament law, as well as things we are most familiar with throughout the whole creation—from heaven and earth, and from man himself. These doctrines are shown to be well-grounded upon the irrefutable <note>original: "uncontroulable"</note> principles of widely accepted <note>original: "confessed"</note> theology and philosophy.

The Apostle Paul preached to the Athenians the knowledge of that God whom they worshipped without knowing him <note>See Acts 17:23.</note>. In the same way, these writings aim to teach us, through clear demonstration, things that we—like the Athenians—are too ignorant of. It is written, “God is one” <note>See Deuteronomy 6:4.</note>, which is certainly as true as the passage that says, “There are three that bear witness in heaven” <note>original: "three that bear record in heaven"; a reference to the "Johannine Comma" in 1 John 5:7, a verse historically used to support the doctrine of the Trinity.</note>.

[God is also] said to be light and love, and in him is no darkness at all; he is meek, merciful, of everlasting kindness, and fury is not in him.

It is also written that God swore "by two unchangeable <note>original: "immutable"</note> things, in which it was impossible for God to lie" (Hebrews 6:18). And because he could swear by no one greater, he swore by himself: "That in blessing I would bless Abraham; and curse him that cursed Abraham" (Genesis 12:3). Here, two principles are clearly expressed, and both are called unchangeable: one is wrath, fury, and a consuming fire; the other is love, meekness, and an indescribable <note>original: "ineffable"</note> light.

Had humanity remained in its original <note>original: "primitive"</note> state, totally surrendered <note>original: "resigned"</note> to divine love, we would not have known the principle of wrath. Instead, it would have remained more hidden than the bitterness of the root is within the most pleasant fruit. The writings of Jacob Behmen <note>original: "Jacob Behmen," a common 17th-century English spelling of Jakob Böhme (1575–1624), the German Christian mystic</note> and the texts that follow are concerned with this work and wisdom. The way to benefit from reading these writings is to be filled with patience, keeping our minds in a state of singleness and simplicity. In this frame of mind, we should seek diligently, pray earnestly, and entirely surrender our wills to the guidance and direction of Divine Love.

**Ens** <note>original: "Ens," Latin for "being" or "existence."</note> is the proper and unique <note>original: "peculiar"</note> being or essence—whether good or evil—of a thing.

**Eternal Nature** belongs to <note>original: "is of"</note> the first principle. Within it exist <note>original: "standeth"</note> two kingdoms (which have been manifested since the fall of Lucifer). One is the pure virgin wisdom of God: the single holy element as it was first created, which is the cause of the four elements and the stars. The other is the severe, fierce wrath, because of which God calls himself a "consuming fire." It is, therefore, the "Great Mystery" <note>In Böhme’s philosophy, the *Mysterium Magnum* is the chaos or "abyss" from which the world was created.</note>.

**Expressed Word** <note>In Böhme’s theology, this refers to the "Verbum Fiat," the spoken word of God that manifests as physical reality.</note> comprises <note>original: "comprizeth"</note> all creatures, both visible and invisible.

**Fiat** <note>original: "Fiat," Latin for "let it be done"; the creative command of God.</note> is the eternal speaking Word or creating power that proceeds from the free will of the abyss. It is also the desire of the soul as it takes on the power of the holy "tincture" <note>In Böhme’s usage, a "tincture" is a spiritual essence or quality that transforms or "colors" a substance.</note> of love and makes it substantial <note>original: "essential"</note>.

*Flagrat* <note>original: "Flagrat," from the Latin *flagrare*, meaning to burn or blaze.</note>, or a sudden fright, terror, shriek, or flash; the severing of the two principles of light and darkness. It resembles thunder and lightning, gunpowder, and so on. It is the pregnant echo of the sound of eternity, speaking love or anger: life in the light, and death in the darkness. This is called the <term>salnitral flagrat</term> <note>original: "salnitral flagrat"; in Boehme's thought, "salniter" refers to a spiritual explosive force, similar to the chemical properties of saltpeter (nitre) used in gunpowder.</note>.

*Idea* is the <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens," Latin for "being" or "essence."</note>, which first exists within man and is then known. But it exists because God sees it.

*Incentive* is a divine spark, held captive so that it can only glimmer, but not shine forth fully.

*Magia* <note>original: "Magia," Latin for magic.</note>: there are two types of <term>Magic</term>, one of the Unity (the Divine) and the other of multiplicity, or <term>astral powers</term>. There are also two <term>Magi</term> <note>original: "Magi," the plural of magus; wise men or practitioners of magic.</note> within man: the Spirit of God and human reason. Into the latter, the devil easily insinuates himself.

*Majesty* is the light of the glorious Son of God, the second principle, according to which alone God is called God; for according to the first principle, he

is not called God, but a consuming fire.

*<term>Lubet</term>* <note>original: "Lubet," likely from the Latin *libet*, meaning "it pleases."</note>, a longing delight or *good pleasure* <note>original: "bene placitum."</note>. In God, it is the free, fertile will toward manifestation in nature and among created beings; without this, everything would have remained in eternal stillness. The *Lubet* in man is the motivating will toward good or evil.

*<term>Great Mystery</term>* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum."</note> is that from which all visible and invisible *created things* <note>original: "concreates"; things created together or existing as a whole.</note> originated; it belongs to the first principle.

*<term>Out-birth</term>*, the visible, tangible, and mortal part of this world, which is called the "anger-fire."

It rises into a *sudden blaze* <note>original: "flagrat," a burst of fire or explosion.</note> out of the sulfurous <note>original: "brimstony."</note>, watery, and earthy properties, through which these properties are unfolded and made clear.

*<term>Satan</term>* refers to the spirit of error. It does not always mean a specific created devil, but rather the quality or property of such an erroneous spirit.

<page-num>23</page-num>

*<term>Salliter</term>* <note>original: "Salliter," a spiritualized concept of saltpeter (nitre), representing an explosive, formative force in nature.</note>, called Divine Salliter. It is represented by the earth but is like the pure heaven. Within it are moving and springing powers that produce all kinds of divine trees, plants, and so on, with heavenly colors, smells, and tastes. The trees, gold, silver, and precious stones of this world are only hard, dead, and dark shadows of it. This Salliter and *<term>Mercurius</term>* <note>original: "mercurius"; in alchemy, the fluid, transformative spirit or messenger between worlds.</note>—that is, divine Mercurius—is the food of angels and holy souls.

*<term>Sophia</term>* <note>original: "Sophia," Greek for "Wisdom."</note>, the true, noble, and precious image of Christ; namely, the wisdom of God and the *<term>tincture</term>* <note>original: "tincture"; a spiritual essence or coloring force that transforms and gives life.</note> of the light.

*Source*, the original quality or essential property of love and anger, light and darkness, according to both time and eternity.

*<term>Ternarius</term>* <note>original: "Ternarius," Latin for "the number three."</note>, or the number three in the language of nature. This refers to the divine birth within the six forms of nature, which are the six seals of God.

*Ternary*, called the *<term>Holy Ternary</term>* <note>original: "Ternarium Sanctum," Latin for "Holy Threefold [Being]" or "Holy Ternary."</note>,

<summary>This page continues the glossary of Jacob Boehme's mystical vocabulary, defining "Salliter" as the spiritual substance of heaven, "Sophia" as divine wisdom, and "Ternarius" as the expression of the Trinity within nature.</summary>
<keywords>Salliter, Sophia, Mercurius, Source, Ternarius, Ternarium Sanctum, Tincture, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

<page-num>24</page-num>

...is holy flesh, or holy substance, or the embodiment <note>original: "corporizing."</note> of angels and holy souls.

*<term>Tincture</term>* <note>original: "Tincture"; in Boehme's alchemy, a spiritual essence or "coloring" force that permeates matter to give it life, beauty, and virtue.</note> is, in angels and glorified saints, the pure fire of love. In humans, it is that which Adam's fall caused almost completely to disappear. In vegetables, animals, and minerals, it is their virtue, vitality, or life, spirit, and power.

*<term>Turba</term>* <note>original: "Turba"; Latin for "uproar" or "crowd." In this context, it refers to a disruptive or destructive force triggered by sin or the "wrath" of nature.</note> is understood as a fierce fire of wrath which destroys a corruptible body. Sometimes it refers to horrific tempests or hellish blasts—general contagions <note>The original text uses "contagious" as a noun, likely referring to widespread pestilence or plagues.</note> awakened by common sins—called the *<term>Great Turba</term>* <note>original: "Turba Magna."</note>. Sometimes it refers to the eternal fire of wrath which swallows up an evil spirit that lacks a body; this may be called the *<term>Greatest Turba</term>* <note>original: "Turba Maxima."</note>.

# CONSIDERATIONS
## BY WAY OF ENQUIRY
### INTO THE
## SUBJECT, MATTER, AND SCOPE
## OF THE WRITINGS
### OF THE
## DIVINELY-ILLUMINATED
# JACOB BOEHME <note>original: "Jacob Behmen."</note>.

->*Introduction.*<-

Since we are now to speak of God—what He is and where He is—we must say that God Himself is the essence of all essences <note>original: "essence of all essences"; Boehme's term for God as the fundamental reality or "source-being" from which all other things derive their existence.</note>. For all things are generated or born, created, and proceed from Him, and all things take their first beginning out of God, as the Scriptures testify, saying:

> “Through Him and in Him are all things. Also, the heaven and the heaven of heavens are not able to contain You. Also, heaven is My throne, and the earth is My footstool.” And regarding the Father, it is mentioned: “Yours is the kingdom, and the power”—meaning all power.

But there is still this difference to be observed: that evil is neither God, nor is it called God;

<meta>catchword: God;</meta>

<summary>This introductory page establishes God as the fundamental source and "essence of all essences" from which all creation proceeds, while clarifying that evil is distinct from the divine nature.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, divinity, essence of essences, Scripture, omnipresence, nature of God, origin of all things</keywords>

--- Page 172 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Considerations" from the previous page, further exploring the relationship between God's essence and the nature of the created world, specifically addressing the origin of wrath and the "threefold form" of the spirit.</meta>

<page-num>26</page-num>
<header>Considerations on the Scope</header>
<meta>catchword: Now</meta>

God; and though the first principle in nature has [by the fall of Lucifer <note>original: "Lucifer"; in Boehme’s theology, the fall of this archangel corrupted the fundamental forces of nature, turning what was meant to be strength into "wrath."</note> become] the earnest fountain of wrathfulness—according to which God calls Himself an angry, wrathful, and zealous God, and which is the original cause of life and of all movement—yet if that principle is irradiated with the light of God, it is no longer wrath or harshness <note>original: "tartness"; refers to the sharp, stinging quality of the first principles of nature before they are softened by divine light.</note>, but is changed into great joy and love.

Now when God created the world and all things in it, He had no other matter or materials to make it from except what proceeded immediately from His own being, essence, or substance, out of Himself. But God is a spirit that is incomprehensible, which has neither beginning nor end, and His greatness and depth are everything. Yet a spirit does nothing but ascend, flow, move, and continually generate itself in a **threefold form**: namely, bitterness, harshness, and heat. 

These three types of forms are none of them first, second, or third; for all three are but one, and each of them begets, bears, and brings forth the other two. For a spirit is like a will or a thought, which rising up, beholds, perfects, and generates itself.

<summary>This page explains that creation originated from God's own substance and describes the "threefold form" (bitterness, harshness, and heat) of the divine spirit, which can manifest as either wrath or love depending on its relationship to divine light.</summary>

--- Page 173 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Considerations" from the previous page, further exploring the relationship between God's essence and the nature of the created world. It specifically addresses how human language struggles to describe God's eternal nature and the origin of opposing forces like love and wrath.</meta>

<page-num>27</page-num>
<header>Of Jacob Boehme's <note>original: "Behmen's"; Jacob Boehme (1575–1624) was a German Christian mystic whose complex ideas about the nature of God and the origin of evil influenced many later philosophers.</note> Writings.</header>

Now this cannot be fully expressed or described, nor brought to human understanding through language; for God has no beginning. However, it is set down here as if He had a beginning, in order to distinguish the first principle from the second principle, and to explain what God or Spirit is. Truly, there is no division within God; nevertheless, when we ask where good and evil come from, we must understand the original source of wrath and anger, as well as the source of love. Both of these proceed from the same origin—from one "mother"—even though they are a single reality within the divine unity.

It cannot be said that fire, bitterness, and harshness exist within God, much less that air, water, or earth are in Him. We only know that all these things have proceeded out of that original source. Nor can it be said that death, hellfire, or sorrow exist in God; rather, it is known that these things have come out of the original source. These things exist within "eternal nature," and eternal nature originated indirectly from God. Therefore, the root cause must be sought: specifically, what is the "first matter" of evil? This is what has manifested itself within the spirit of the creature, as well as the wrath of God in the first principle <note>In Boehme’s theology, the "first principle" is the dark, fiery foundation of all existence which, when separated from God's light, becomes wrath and "hell."</note>, which burns within it continually.

<meta>catchword: CHAPTER</meta>

<summary>This page explains that while God is a unified being without division, human language must use distinctions like the "first and second principles" to understand how both divine love and natural wrath originate from the same source. It argues that while evil and physical elements proceed from God's "eternal nature," they do not exist within God’s essential being.</summary>

### CHAPTER I.

#### Concerning the true Principles of Darkness and Light, from which (through the fall of Lucifer) come evil and good, ferocity and gentleness, anger and love.

To begin with things closest to our outward senses—tangible bodies, such as darkness, cold, and so on. These forces condense, crowd, pack together, and give substance to things. Conversely, light and heat rarify <note>To make less dense or more thin.</note>, thin out, make subtle, and activate bodies adapted to them.

Both forces, when in their proper proportion [as they were before the fall of Lucifer <note>In Christian tradition, Lucifer is the high angel whose rebellion and fall from heaven introduced discord and evil into the created world.</note>], were equally good and friendly. But now, at any time or in any place where either becomes extremely dominant and tyrannical, they immediately become hostile <note>original: "inimicitious"</note> and destructive.

Considering this, and what can be deduced from it, might explain why J. A. Comenius <note>John Amos Comenius (1592–1670), a Moravian philosopher and educator often called the "father of modern education."</note> was offended by Campanella <note>Tommaso Campanella (1568–1639), an Italian Dominican friar and philosopher whose works attempted to reconcile science and religion.</note> for basing...

<meta>catchword: ...ing</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the dual principles of light and darkness, arguing that while they were originally balanced and good, their imbalance following the fall of Lucifer created the destructive forces of evil and wrath. The author also begins to reference a philosophical disagreement between the scholars Comenius and Campanella regarding these principles.</summary>
