Now, although no one can say how high the eighth sphere <note>In the traditional Ptolemaic model of the universe, the eighth sphere was the realm of the fixed stars, situated beyond the seven planetary spheres and representing the boundary of the visible heavens.</note> is—much less the infinity beyond or outside of it—every person can still measure their own height, as they are truly a part of it. So, as far as can be reached by such a lowly, insignificant creature <note>original: "vile a nothing"</note>, some small portion of the abyss of grace <note>abyss: a translation of Böhme’s concept of the *Ungrund*, the bottomless, boundless nature of God's grace and being.</note> may be articulated, even if only in stammers. Perhaps some assistance may be given through this to those seekers whose inner senses are opened in some degree to receive such sublime truths, and whose spirits are humbled by the modest way in which they are presented.

All knowledge that does not lead us humbly toward *Jesus Christ* is actually ignorance. Compared with the light set within us by Him, such knowledge is darkness. Compared with the power <note>original: "virtue"</note> of that light, it is worthless <note>original: "dung"; a reference to the Apostle Paul's words in Philippians 3:8.</note>. Compared with the holiness and purity of that light, it is filthiness. Compared with the order of that light, it is confusion and a distraction <note>original: "amusement of spirit," which in this period meant a state of being bewildered or diverted from the truth.</note> of the spirit. And compared with the benefit and usefulness of the glorious light of *Christ*, it is a mere waste of precious time.

In the previous discourse, we have seen (to some extent) the shameful state into which *Adam* fell by losing the divine virgin of God's wisdom <note>Böhme personifies God's Wisdom as the "Divine Sophia," a heavenly counterpart or "virgin" that Adam was said to have lost during the Fall, leading to his spiritual degradation.</note>, with whom he should have lived forever in puri-

# Considerations on the Scope

<meta>catchword: them-</meta>

It should be noted that all heathens <note>original: "Heathen"; a term used historically for non-Christian people or those outside the Judeo-Christian tradition.</note> have the gospel preached to them, more or less, though it is present obscurely within them. For just as they—without the written Law—do the things contained in the Law, so they also—without the written Gospel—partake in the mercy proclaimed by the Gospel.

That is to say, being conscious of their own faults, they observe that they receive good in return for evil. This is the virtue of the "second principle" <note>In the theology of Jacob Böhme, the "second principle" represents the divine realm of light, love, and the Gospel, as opposed to the "first principle" of fire and wrath.</note>, or the Gospel of the Word written throughout all of creation—seen in the "smiles of the heavens" during fruitful seasons and in the bountiful produce of the earth. Despite this, if they harden their hearts, they become sinners against both Law and Gospel: the Law written in their hearts and the Gospel shining in the light of their understanding. This is so conspicuous that, although some of them laughed at the news of the resurrection, we read that they were serious at the news of the judgment to come, as every one of them has a self-condemning judge in his own heart.

Love is as strong as death, but this love in Christ is much stronger. Sinful and finally unrepentant <note>original: "impenitent"</note> men, hell, and devils only judge 
them—

# CHAPTER XXI.

## On the incarnation of the Lord Jesus Christ.

*First consideration.* It ought to be attentively pondered that when the fullness of time, or the limit of the covenant, had come, Jesus took on the whole of human nature from the Virgin Mary. But because she was the daughter of Joachim and Anna, and a descendant of Eve, she could not be a virgin of total purity <note>In this theological context, the author is arguing that Mary, as a human descendant of Eve, shared in the "unclean" or fallen nature of humanity rather than being born naturally sinless.</note> by her own nature:
> “Who can 
> bring a clean thing out of an unclean? 
> Not one.” Job 14:4.

*Second consideration.* Jesus Christ neither came into a clean vessel, nor took a pure nature upon himself [from the start], but instead he made the vessel clean, and caused the polluted nature to become pure. It was a misunderstanding of this fact that led the *Collyridians* <note>original: "Colliridiani"; a 4th-century sect, primarily composed of women, who were declared heretics for worshipping the Virgin Mary as a goddess and offering her sacrificial cakes.</note> to offer divine worship to the Virgin Mary.

*Third consideration.* We must understand, then, that when our Redeemer took our nature upon

<summary>This page explores the theology of the Incarnation, arguing that Jesus inherited a fallen human nature through the Virgin Mary but purified it through his own divine presence rather than entering a pre-purified vessel.</summary>
<keywords>Incarnation, Jesus Christ, Virgin Mary, Joachim and Anna, Collyridians, Job 14:4, original sin, purification</keywords>

--- Page 277 ---
<meta>This page continues the numbered "considerations" regarding the Incarnation of Christ, following the statement on the previous page that our Redeemer took our nature upon himself.</meta>

<page-num>131</page-num>
<header>Of JACOB BEHMEN'S Writings.</header>

upon him, he took it from all of the Virgin Mary's essences <note>original: "essences"; in the theology of Jacob Behmen (Jakob Böhme), these are the fundamental spiritual qualities or "being" of a person.</note>: those of her soul (derived from the first principle), those of her spirit (derived from the second), and those of her body (derived from the third principle). <note>Behmen’s "Three Principles" represent: 1) The Father/Fire/Will; 2) The Son/Light/Love; and 3) The Outward World/Nature.</note> All of these—soul, body, and spirit—were passed down to her by her immediate parents, Joachim and Anna.

*Fourth consideration.* We must understand that the Lord did not destroy those essences, for he did not come to destroy; rather, he took them exactly as they were. Through the divine "new man" which he brought with him—and which he himself was—he refined and sanctified them, working within them for their salvation and our eternal life.

*Fifth consideration.* We cannot say that there was no evil in the root of the essences he took from the Virgin Mary, as mentioned above. However, his divine conception—exempting him from even the slightest impression, stain, or spot of sin—clarified and enlightened <note>original: "illustrate"; here meaning to make luminous or clear, as if by light.</note> them. This process is like fire transforming iron, a tincture <note>original: "tincture"; in alchemy and Behmen's thought, a spiritual force or substance that can transform and perfect base matter.</note> altering metals, or the sun ripening the harsh, sour, and poor qualities of fruit.

*Sixth consideration.* We should not doubt whether the Lord took a human soul from the Virgin or not—a point about which many have erred;

<page-num>132</page-num>

...erred. For how else could the essences <note>original: "essences"; the fundamental spiritual qualities of a person's being.</note> of our poor, captivated souls have been rescued, if the "second Adam" <note>A biblical title for Jesus Christ (see 1 Corinthians 15:45), contrasted with the first Adam who brought sin into the world.</note> had not assumed a human soul as truly as that which the first Adam possessed? It was our souls that were held by sin and death and needed redemption; therefore, our Lord made his soul—which was a true, real human soul—the offering for sin.

*Seventh consideration.* Nor should we fear that the Lord failed to take our mortal flesh and blood upon himself, or that he brought "heavenly flesh" during his conception and possessed only that. For he also took our true, real body of flesh and blood from the Virgin Mary. He took our weak, frail body, which was subject to the physical vulnerabilities <note>original: "passions"</note> of hunger, sleep, and so on. Within this body lay the weight of divine wrath and the curse, all of which he bore along with the sufferings that came with it: temptations, lashings, insults, piercings, crucifixion, anguish, death, and burial. He then raised that very same body, which gives us the assurance of our own resurrection and opens a door for our bodies (though they are now mortal) to share eternal life along with the soul.

*Eighth consideration.* We must understand that our new bodies will be the very same true, real bodies we have now, and not—

<meta>This page continues the "eighth consideration" regarding the nature of the resurrected body, arguing that it remains the same substance as the original body despite undergoing spiritual transformation.</meta>

—other bodies. Though they do not consist of "accidents" <note>original: "accidents"; in philosophical and theological terms, these are non-essential qualities (like color or size) that can change without changing the underlying substance of a thing.</note> and are not touchable <note>original: "palpable"</note>, they are still substantial, as is shown by the following demonstrations:

**First demonstration.** The changes <note>original: "mutations"</note> in nature are not a destruction of bodies. For example, when a grain of wheat is sown, it undergoes several changes, yet it remains the same. Although it first "dies," it is only broken down by the Separator <note>original: "Separator"; a term used by Böhme to describe the natural or spiritual force that divides, organizes, and re-composes matter.</note> into the various parts of its own composition. This happens so that it may be capable of blending and uniting other compatible substances <note>original: "assimilated bodies"</note> to itself. In this way, it gathers an increase of matter, which it admits only in the right proportion to become its own "father and mother"—to grow and multiply itself. Yet it is still wheat, even though it has increased in quantity into many grains of wheat; just as an oak tree is merely a perfected acorn.

**Second demonstration.** Nature moves forward in an orderly motion and will not return to its hidden source <note>original: "mystery"</note> by the same steps it took to emerge until it has completed its full cycle <note>original: "circulation"</note>. You might as well try to turn the sun back toward the east as try to make a fruit become a tree, except through its orderly motion: first into a fruit and seed, and then the old is renewed in the new, rather than becoming something else entirely. So is the new...

<sig>K 5</sig>
<meta>catchword: body,</meta>

<summary>Böhme uses analogies from nature, such as a grain of wheat and an acorn, to demonstrate that the resurrection of the body is a transformation and renewal of the original substance rather than the creation of a completely different body.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Behmen, resurrection, substance, accidents, Separator, nature, wheat analogy, acorn analogy, mutations</keywords>

body, though glorious, does not exist except through the dying of the old.

**Third Demonstration.** Just as Adam’s fall did not make him a different man, but left him as the same person in another (monstrous) image—and as we are also deformed by that dreadful <note>original: "direful"</note> fall—so too the Lord Jesus, by raising up his own dead body and clothing it with glory and immortality, gave us a resurrection to be like him. This does not make us into different people than we are now. Rather, it happens through the loss of our monstrosity and excess <note>original: "access"; likely referring to the "accidental" or corrupt additions to human nature caused by the fall</note>, and the recovery of the glory we were originally created in, which he has graciously purchased for us.

**Ninth Consideration.** We must understand that when the Lord Jesus Christ took on flesh and blood in the Virgin’s womb, he was not only present there, but was also then established and reigning within the entire infinite abyss of the Father.

**Tenth Consideration.** We must also understand that after the divine nature, or the infinite Godhead of Jesus Christ, had taken on human nature, he remained the same unmixed, glorious Second Person <note>The Second Person of the Trinity: God the Son</note> as he was before; and yet, by taking on humanity—

humanity from the Virgin Mary, he really became what he was not before: namely, a real Son of Man, with a true human soul, body, and spirit, just as we are, and capable of suffering and dying.

**Eleventh consideration.** We should understand that in his ascension and glorification, Christ's human nature (his creaturely state) was not swallowed up by his divine nature, in the way a candle's flame is extinguished by the sun. Instead, that human nature, now glorified, remains forever a glorified body. It is the same true, individual human nature—the same man—who was crucified, died, was buried, and was raised by his almighty power. He is the "first fruit" <note>original: "first fruit"; a biblical reference to Christ being the first of many to be resurrected</note>, the origin, and the author of the general resurrection of all people.

Thus, he was a true heavenly Man, a "virgin" of holiness, purity, and modesty, and was begotten by the Holy Spirit. # Chapter 22

## Concerning the Heavenly New Man, or the Divine Humanity of Jesus Christ; What It Is, and How We May Participate in It.

It is written that you should put off the old man <note>original: "old man"; referring to the fallen, sinful nature of humanity</note> and be renewed, and that you should put on the new man, which (according to God's likeness) is created in righteousness and true holiness (Ephesians 4:22, 23, 24).

**Question:** What is this new man that we are to put on?

**Answer:** It is Jesus Christ. As it is written, "Put on the Lord Jesus Christ," (Romans 13:14). Similarly, we are said to be "clothed" with our house which is from heaven, called a "building of God" (2 Corinthians 5:1, 2). We also find references to being "clothed with humility," "clothed with honor," and "clothed with shame" (Psalm 104:1, 109:29). These are intended not merely as an outer covering, but as a total transformation <note>original: "transmutation"</note> of us from one state to its opposite—much like the woman "clothed with the sun" (Revelation 12:1), where her divine light exists in place of her darkness.

<meta>Question:</meta>

<summary>This page introduces Chapter 22, defining the "New Man" as the divine humanity of Jesus Christ which believers must "put on." It explains that being "clothed" in Christ signifies a total internal transformation from a state of darkness to one of divine light.</summary>

<keywords>New Man, Divine Humanity, Transformation, Jesus Christ, Ephesians 4, Romans 13, Spiritual Clothing, Transmutation</keywords>

**Question:** What aspect of Christ may we "put on" and be clothed with, and what is it that we are to be "clothed upon" with?

**First answer (negatively):** Not His almightiness <note>original: "almightiness"; referring to His divine omnipotence</note>; for His omnipotence is something that no creature can behold or reach. So too is the brightness of His Father’s glory, which upholds all things by the word of His power.

<margin>|</margin>
**Second answer (negatively):** Nor the body, soul, and spirit He received from the Virgin Mary, which was earthly—a body, soul, and spirit descending from Eve. Because He was born of the flesh of Mary, that part of Him was flesh. Since He humbled Himself to take on this form, He was the Son of Man and our brother. Therefore, we do not "put Him on" in that specific sense, for our Lord remains in that form; that humanity which He received from the Virgin remains a created thing (Acts 1:11), and is not so mixed with the divine as to be confused with or swallowed up by the Deity <note>original: "Deity"; the divine nature or essence of God</note>.

The two men in white <note>referring to the angels present at the Ascension</note> testified that He would descend in the same way they had seen Him ascend. See our demonstration 11, in the specific section of the chapter on the Incarnation.

**Third answer (affirmatively):** Instead, the "New Man" is the heavenly new man, or the heavenly...

The first Adam had, in a sense, rejected <note>original: "repudiated"</note> this holy image. Let no one wonder that my weak hand seeks strength and delights to speak of this; for this is that dear, precious image of virginity, purity, and modesty. Its eternal, perfect, and sweet love was exchanged by the fallen <note>original: "apostate"</note> Adam for the lust of a woman, which soon led him into mortality.

Just as the sun is to the outward world, this image would have been man's guide, emerging herself from the depths <note>original: "abyss"</note> of infinite Wisdom. This is what the promised seed <note>referring to Christ as the fulfillment of the promise in Genesis 3:15</note> brings with Him,

This heavenly humanity, which disappeared when Adam fell, provides the water of life that becomes a well springing up to eternal life. This is the "children’s bread" <note>a term for spiritual nourishment intended for the faithful</note>; it fills Paradise, feeds the angels, and sustains all the blessed forever.

This is the quintessence <note>the purest or most concentrated form of a substance; the "fifth essence" beyond the four physical elements</note> of all things—the holy element from which the four physical elements streamed. It is the life and power <note>original: "virtue"</note> of eternal nature, or the "Great Mystery" <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; Jacob Behmen's term for the source of all existence and the hidden nature of God</note>. It is that pearl of great price which, whoever finds and buys it, has found a bargain, no matter what it costs them.

This is Christ’s heavenly flesh and blood, with which he feeds his followers in the sacramental feast <note>the Lord's Supper or Eucharist</note>. But having now come to a subject that people often use as an occasion for controversy, it may be helpful to discuss it further.

### Of the Lord’s Supper.

In John 6, from verse 47 to 59, and again in verse 63, it is said: "I am the living bread that came down from heaven." Verse 53 says: "Unless you eat his flesh and drink his blood, you have no life in you."

The Jews—understanding this to mean his outward, physical flesh and blood, as many people still do—asked, and quite rightly from their perspective: "How can this man give us his flesh to eat?" For they understood it in a cannibal-like way, which was not at all what our Lord intended.

For he explains his own meaning, first negatively in verse 63: he did not mean his physical flesh, as they thought, for that "profits nothing" <note>original: "profiteth nothing"; implying that physical matter alone cannot grant eternal life</note>. And affirmatively, in verses 50 and 58, the Lord is explicit that the flesh he meant was also bread, and that bread was the bread which came down from heaven,

<meta>catchword: heaven,</meta>

<summary>The author explains that Christ's references to eating his flesh and blood in the Gospel of John refer to a spiritual "heavenly bread" rather than physical, outward flesh, addressing common misunderstandings of the Lord's Supper.</summary>
<keywords>Lord's Supper, Eucharist, John 6, spiritual nourishment, heavenly bread, sacramental feast, Jacob Behmen</keywords>

--- Page 289 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of "heavenly bread" and the "word of God" as the true substance of Christ, contrasting it with physical flesh.</meta>

<page-num>143</page-num>
<header>Of Jacob Behmen’s Writings.</header>

heaven, namely, “The word of God.” From this, two controversial viewpoints arise that differ from each other, and both differ from the truth.

**The first error:** One party says they eat the outward physical flesh, which once eaten, they swallow into their animal bodies <note>original: “beastial carcase”</note>. The other party opposes this, saying that flesh—even though it is now glorified—remains a physically limited <term>circumscriptive creature</term> <note>a created thing confined to a specific place or boundary</note>; therefore, it cannot be eaten everywhere at the same time. And since it is only one body and not many, they argue it cannot be eaten at all.

**The second error:** This latter party, in opposition to the former, further claims that they eat that flesh and drink that blood only through faith or imagination. However, they know nothing of the heavenly flesh or humanity and still only understand the concept as referring to outward, physical flesh. Yet the Lord is explicit about this, saying, “It is the Spirit that gives life <note>original: “quickeneth”</note>; the flesh profits nothing.” He says that his words “are spirit and life,” and that he “is that bread which came,” or “comes down from heaven.”

The “mouth” and the “eating” performed by the soul are real and substantial; although they are not physically touchable <note>original: “palpable”</note>, they are not merely imaginary.

<summary>The author identifies two errors regarding the Eucharist: the belief in eating literal physical flesh and the belief that it is purely symbolic or imaginary. He argues that the soul truly and substantially feeds on Christ's heavenly, spiritual substance.</summary>

### What is and what is not received in the Lord’s Supper.

It is not the outward flesh and blood they ate and drank, for he was sitting with them when they ate and drank; this shows plainly that the sacramental flesh and blood is the divine flesh and blood that came down from heaven.

In this, Enoch walked with God and fed so heartily on this food that the food of the <term>third principle</term> <note>original: "third principle"; in the philosophy of Jacob Boehme, this refers to the physical, material world.</note> could no longer satisfy him; this new wine made the bottle <note>A reference to Jesus's parable of new wine in new wineskins; here it refers to Enoch's body being renewed.</note> new. This, like the <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "tincture"; an alchemical term for a spiritual essence or catalyst capable of transforming or "transmuting" matter into a higher state.</note> that transforms metals, inflamed and translated his whole being, so that his very <term>third principle</term> (physical nature) was swallowed up by the divine <term>second principle</term> <note>original: "second principle"; refers to the eternal world of Light and the divine nature of Christ.</note>.

By this, all the holy men who lived before Christ’s incarnation were nourished during their pilgrimage on earth, and by this, they were carried home to their native country <note>meaning heaven.</note>.

# CHAPTER XXIII.

## Man's mistaken way towards a supposed happiness.

The state of the children of Adam <note>Humanity in its fallen state.</note> is the same as those described by the prophet Hosea:
> "Behold, I will hedge
> up your ways with thorns, and build
> a wall, so that she shall not find her paths;
> and she shall follow after her lovers, but
> she shall not overtake them; and she
> shall seek them, but she shall not find
> them. Then shall she say, 'I will go
> and return to my first husband; for
> then it was better with me than now,'"

Hosea 2:6, 7. And God proceeds against the <term>apostate</term> <note>Someone who has abandoned their religious faith or principles.</note> adulteress to the end of verse 13; but in verse 14, and through the end of the chapter, he returns her to himself, etc.

Wise men in every generation have searched within the treasures of creation for real happiness and rest. And after they have dived into the deep; ransacked the many mysteries...

### The way to true wisdom.

Our lesson is to become fools regarding the wisdom of this world and, as such, to crucify our wills against the lusts of the eye, the lusts of the flesh, and the pride of life <note>These are the three categories of worldly temptation mentioned in 1 John 2:16</note>. We should be like little children who desire nothing but the fulfillment of their basic needs and, without contract, bargain, or regret, do what the Father <note>referring to God</note> commands—His will being their guiding principle. 

As a reward for their resigned <note>original: "resigned"; in this context, it means surrendered or submissively aligned with God's will</note> obedience, they know no perplexing cares, frightening anxieties <note>original: "forecasting fears"</note>, crafty excuses, nor deception <note>original: "dissimulation"</note>, malice, etc. Instead, they have a calm, serene face, a peaceable, contented mind, and a cheerful, innocent behavior while awake; and when they lie down, they fall sweetly asleep.

## CHAPTER XXIV.

### The Lord’s way for man to follow on his return home.

But man has traveled, like the prodigal <note>original: "prodigal"; a reference to the Parable of the Prodigal Son in Luke 15, referring to someone who has wastefully wandered away from God</note>, away from this pure <note>original: "white state"; representing a state of innocence or spiritual clarity</note> condition. To the person who is straying, will he allow someone who sincerely desires the welfare of souls to say: "Stop; stand still and consider if a good end is not better than a bad one." A good path must be better than an evil one, for the evil path leads to a bad end.

->Arguments against death<- <note>original: "Dissuasives from death"; arguments intended to deter someone from a path leading to spiritual death</note>

First, to be satisfied whether this path is indeed bad, let him look closely, heedfully, and impartially into his own heart and inclinations. There he shall find a core of self-love desiring to possess everything. This comes from the first form <note>In Böhme’s complex mystical system, the "seven forms" or "properties" are the fundamental forces of eternal nature; the "first form" is the initial movement toward existence</note>, namely, astringency <note>original: "astringency"; in Böhme's philosophy, this is the harsh, contracting force of the first property that pulls everything inward toward the self</note>.

Secondly, he shall find an eager desire to be exalted and to be great above others;

<meta>catchword: which</meta>

<summary>This page exhorts the reader to pause and examine their inner life, identifying the spiritual roots of self-love and pride as the "first form" of nature’s contraction. It serves as a call for the "straying" soul to recognize its departure from a state of innocence and begin the journey back to God.</summary>

<keywords>Jakob Böhme, spiritual journey, self-examination, self-love, pride, astringency, first form, prodigal son, mysticism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the analysis of Jakob Böhme’s "seven forms of nature" within the human soul. Having discussed the first form (astringency or self-love) on the previous page, it now addresses the second, third, and fourth forms as sources of human sin and spiritual disorder.</meta>

<header>Of Jacob Behmen’s <note>original: "Jacob Behmen"; the common English spelling for the German mystic Jakob Böhme (1575–1624)</note> Writings.</header>

Which pride comes from the second form of nature: attraction or constriction <note>original: "constringency"; the second of Böhme's seven properties, representing the pulling together or drawing in that creates tension and movement</note>.

Thirdly, he shall perceive a contempt, a despising, and an envying of others: he thinks he exceeds them in worth, while they exceed him in fortune or the like, which causes him to fret and complain <note>original: "repines"</note>. This envy proceeds from the third form of nature: anguish <note>In Böhme's system, the "third form" is a restless, turning motion or "anguish" caused by the conflict between the first two forces (contraction and expansion)</note>.

Fourthly, he shall then find violent anger, rage, and fury when he is crossed or restrained by anyone. This comes from the fourth form of nature: fire <note>The "fourth form" is the flash or heat that occurs when the dark, contracting forces meet the light, often manifesting in the fallen soul as wrath or anger</note>.

And under these four headings are troops of sins and additions <note>original: "appendixes"</note> related to them. When these are well-considered, they may make every child of Adam <note>A traditional term for all of humanity, as descendants of the first man in the Bible</note> cry out as the blessed Paul did:

> “O wretched man that I am! Who shall 
> “deliver me from the body of this 
> “death?” Romans 7:24.

He may now see how evil these disorders and hellish principles are in themselves, and how evil they are for him. He can see how dangerous it is to establish a government in his own private, individual soul that is opposite to the one entire, gracious, universal, and omnipotent will and government of God.

For just as Adam did, whenever we continue to insert our own wills into the third principle <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "third principle" is the visible, material world or "outward nature"</note>, we are like gloomy eclipses, acting in hostility against the outward flows of heaven’s light.

Adam lost, and we also lose, the heavenly virgin of purity and modesty by discovering and living according to the desires <note>original: "lust"</note> of the third principle <note>In Jakob Böhme’s philosophy, the "third principle" refers to the visible, material world governed by the elements and the stars</note>. This involves following a deceptive wisdom that leads us to seek a mistaken fulfillment in pleasure, profit, and honor within this elemental, physical <note>original: "palpable"</note>, temporary, and astral world.

When a person makes these things the idolatrous objects of their love, hope, delight, and confidence—just as the rich man in the Gospel did <note>An allusion to the "Rich Fool" or the "Rich Young Ruler" in the teachings of Jesus (e.g., Luke 12:16–21)</note>—they are clinging to things that should be used only as a picture or a mirror <note>original: "glass"</note>. Through these physical things, we are meant to contemplate, by divine help, the eternal powers they represent. Instead, such a person catches at a shadow and worships the donkey on which he was supposed to ride.

### The nature of the death that brings true life.

It is evident that living for the third principle was the cause of Adam’s death to the second principle <note>The "second principle" represents the divine world of light, love, and the kingdom of God</note>—and it remains the cause of our own loss of that divine life. It is equally clear that "dying" to the third principle is the only way to recover the life that Adam lost. This "dying" means allowing the material world to hang upon us only like an old, loose garment, or looking through it as one looks through a cloud or a medium to see the sun.

<meta>catchword: Thus</meta>

<summary>This page explains that Adam's fall resulted from prioritizing the material world (the "third principle") over spiritual purity. It argues that true spiritual life is recovered by "dying" to worldly desires and treating the physical world as a mere reflection of eternal truths.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, third principle, second principle, heavenly virgin, spiritual death, materialism, Jakob Böhme, idolatry</keywords>

Thus, to use the third principle <note>In Böhme's philosophy, the "third principle" refers to the visible, material world governed by the elements</note> only in this way is to live for it correctly; that is, to truly die to it. And to die to it in this way is to truly live for the second principle <note>The "second principle" represents the divine world of light, love, and the kingdom of God</note> and to be reunited with the eternal, chaste, holy, modest, and pure virgin <note>This "virgin" represents Sophia, or Divine Wisdom, who Böhme believed withdrew from humanity when Adam fell into selfish desire</note> who disappeared upon Adam's disobedient desire <note>original: "lusting"</note>. For such desire awakens the knowledge of evil and good, which the virgin abhors and retires from.

And lest humanity should be quite estranged, the Son of the virgin came to rescue us from this miserable toil. His doctrine teaches us that it is a greater blessing to give than to receive. This is because the holy world of light—which consists of the power and virtue of the second principle—cannot be supported or receive nourishment from the divided properties of the third principle, which consists of temporary, changeable powers. Whatever is good within those outward-flowing powers is the free gift from the abyss <note>original: "abyss," used here to mean the infinite, bottomless source or "unground" of God</note> of that true and greatest blessedness: the second principle.

All, therefore, who do not die to the lesser blessing cannot live for the greater blessing. If we do not die to all things but Christ, we are unworthy of him. Whatever is divided from him is dead, for he alone is life—and

<meta>This page continues the theological exploration of Jakob Böhme’s "Three Principles." It contrasts God’s nature as a "consuming fire" in the first principle with His nature as "light and love" in the second, while describing the spiritual "death" required to move from the material third principle into the life of Christ.</meta>

<page-num>156</page-num>
<header>Considerations on the Scope</header>

God is called "God" according to His infinite **abyss** <note>original: "abyfs"; here referring to the "Unground" or the bottomless, infinite source of all being</note> of light, love, life, goodness, and the **second principle** <note>In Böhme’s system, the "second principle" is the divine world of light and love, personified in the Son</note>. For according to the **first principle** <note>The "first principle" represents God's raw power, fire, and potential for wrath, associated with the Father’s stern justice</note>, He is—and in Scripture is called—a consuming fire.

A person living for the deceptive pleasures of the **third principle** <note>The "third principle" is the visible, material world governed by the four elements and temporary nature</note> is spiritually dead, just as a widow who lives for pleasure is said to be dead even while she lives.

We are shocked at the thought of being adulterers or idolaters, yet that is exactly what we are as long as we live for ourselves—that is, until we die with Christ.

We remain in this state until we pass through death; that is, until we truly and really die to the wrath, fierceness, and dark anguish of the first principle, where the root of the soul resides as part of the "Father’s property" <note>The "Father's property" refers to the soul's natural foundation in divine power and fire, which must be transformed by the light of the Son</note>. 

We must also die to the deceitful, unstable, and bewitching desires <note>original: "lust"</note> of the third principle, into which man’s outward body has fallen (sharing the same state as the animals), and where the curse also resides. **UNTIL THIS IS DONE**, we are dead to God. 

But by being **mortified** <note>term: mortified; the spiritual practice of "killing off" or subduing the sinful nature and worldly desires</note> to both of these, we are raised again in the same degree to live the life of Christ. This is a life of love, humility, patience, cheerful submission, and obedience. We expand, ascend, or obtain this life in exact proportion to our progress in the spiritual death mentioned above.

<summary>The text explains that God manifests as love in the "second principle" but as a consuming fire in the "first principle." It argues that true spiritual life requires "mortifying" or dying to both the dark fire of the soul's root and the deceptive pleasures of the material world.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, first principle, second principle, third principle, mortification, spiritual death, abyss, consuming fire, life of Christ</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Jakob Böhme's spiritual theology, specifically how "dying" to the first and third principles allows a person to be filled with the divine will and the life of Christ.</meta>

<page-num>157</page-num>
<header>Of JAKOB BÖHME’s <note>original: "JACOB BEHMEN’s"; Böhme (1575–1624) was a German mystic whose name was often anglicized as Behmen</note> *Writings.*</header>

For dying to these two principles <note>Referring to the first principle (the soul's raw power/wrath) and the third principle (the material world and its desires) described on the previous page</note>, so that they no longer dominate us, is such an emptying of ourselves that it enables us to pray, without hesitation, for the Lord to carry out His entire will in and through us. In doing so, we pay no attention to whether that will seems great or humble, sweet or bitter, pleasant or harsh. Instead, we value everything without overthinking or stalling; every divine appointment <note>original: "dispensation"</note> is equally acceptable to us, whether the divine will directs us to act or to endure.

And this life of sacrifice is filled with thankful reverence. It is as complete as the ancient burnt offerings were, held back by no personal reservations. This life is no longer our own, for we have first died to ourselves; instead, it is the life of Christ—the humble, childlike life taught to us by the cross of Christ. The Lord’s footsteps are clear and visible along this good path.

This life exists in both word and power, yet neither the word nor the power belongs to us. It is the holy word and power that Adam abandoned (just as the fallen angels had done before him), and in which the blessed angels communicate and are firmly rooted. It is the same word and power in which Adam might have lived—

But the restored image <note>The "restored image" refers to the divine nature being rebuilt within a person after the Fall of humanity</note> does not grow except under the cross. Thus did Abel live under the hatred of Cain; Enoch and Noah lived under the pressures of the extremely wicked ancient world; and Lot lived among the filthy Sodomites. Most importantly, and infinitely above all others, our Almighty Savior lived this way. If we follow Him, we must do so in the deepest humility, bearing His cross, which must also become ours. We must strip ourselves of all self-pleasing and everything that fallen man claims and considers his own. For this is the holy soul’s "weaning time," when, as heirs of the faith of Abraham, they also celebrate a great feast, just as Abraham did when Isaac was weaned <note>A reference to Genesis 21:8; spiritually, this represents the soul maturing and moving away from the "milk" of basic comforts to a more rigorous, tested faith</note>.

This feast is a true feeding on the heavenly flesh and blood of Christ, and a drinking of the "living water" that becomes a spring within us leading to eternal life. It is a life hidden with Christ in God, a walking with God, and a "putting on" of the Lord Jesus Christ. It is the marriage supper and the wedding garment. It is a peace that surpasses the understanding of everyone except those who are regenerated <note>original: "regenerated"; meaning those who have been spiritually reborn</note>. Ordinarily, this peace is only known to them in proportion to the progress they have made in mortification <note>original: "mortification"; the spiritual practice of subduing the ego and worldly desires—literally "dying" to the self</note>, through which they grow in the

...the new, pure image of God restored to us by Jesus Christ.

In closing, let everyone be warned not to condemn <note>original: "contemn"</note> or despise things they do not understand, and so judge themselves unworthy of this "children's bread" <note>A biblical metaphor from Matthew 15:26, referring to spiritual nourishment and truths intended for the faithful</note>.

The reason why so few understand the Holy Scriptures—which many people read often—is because few surrender their own wills to obey God. Spiritual rebirth <note>original: "regeneration"</note> is the necessary requirement to attain true wisdom.

This is why the writings of the blessed Jacob Behmen <note>Jacob Behmen (1575–1624), also known as Jakob Böhme, was a German Christian mystic whose complex works focused on the nature of God, the fall of man, and the path to salvation</note> remain sealed and silent to a corrupt mind, even if that mind is intellectually sharp. In the same way, the very sermons of the Lord Himself—who spoke as no man ever spoke—were hidden from such people.

### PART THREE

->THE<-

# WORKS

->OF<-

# JAKOB BÖHME <note>original: "Jacob Behmen"; a German mystic and theologian (1575–1624) whose work deeply influenced Western philosophy and Christian mysticism.</note>,

->CONCERNING<-

## GOD and the DIVINE NATURE.

# CHAPTER 1

## Of God and the Divine Nature.

1. Because a divine light is necessary to know and understand this, and because without that divine light the divine essence <term>The fundamental nature or substance of God.</term> cannot be understood at all, I will describe this profound, hidden secret in a way that relates to created beings. By doing so, the reader may reach a deeper understanding; for the divine essence cannot be expressed by speech. It is the spirit of the soul alone that gazes into the true light and understands it.

2. The soul has its origin in God's "first principle" <note>In the author's cosmology, the "First Principle" often refers to the father's fire or the dark foundation of nature from which light emerges.</note> of creation. It was breathed by God into humanity within the "third principle"—that is, into the starry <note>original: "siderial"</note> and elementary birth. Because of this origin, the soul is capable of seeing further into the first principle than any other creature.

<summary>This page introduces the necessity of divine light for understanding God's essence and explains that the human soul, due to its unique creation, has a special capacity to perceive the deep mysteries of God's nature.</summary>
<keywords>divine light, divine essence, soul, first principle, third principle, siderial, elementary birth, mysticism</keywords>

as a disciple of God, originating from and within the very essence from which it proceeded. 3. The devils also see this to an extent, for they also originate from the same first principle. They even wish they could neither see nor feel it; however, it is their own fault that they separated themselves from the second principle—which is called, and truly is, God. # Of God's First Manifestation

## CHAPTER II.
### Of God's first manifestation of himself in the Trinity.

1. God is the will of the wisdom; the wisdom is his manifestation.

2. In this eternal generation <term>The perpetual process by which the Trinity arises within the divine nature.</term>, we are to understand three things: namely, 1. An eternal will. 2. An eternal mind of the will. 3. The egress <term>original: "egreſs"; a "going forth" or outward movement.</term>, efflux <term>original: "efflux"; a flowing out.</term>, or effluence from the will and mind, which is a spirit of the will and mind.

3. The will is the Father. The mind is the conceived comprehension, or receptacle <term>A container or space that holds something.</term> of the will, or the center toward something; it is the will’s heart, which is the Son of God. The egress of the will and mind is the power and spirit.

4. This threefold spirit, or three-in-one, is only one essence—the origin of "the something." Yet it is the mystical mystery, or eternal hiddenness. Just as the human understanding is not confined to time and place, but is its own comprehension and seat, so the egress <note>original: "egress"; here meaning the proceeding or outward flow of the Holy Spirit.</note> of the spirit is the eternal, original contemplation <term>The divine act of self-reflection or vision.</term>.

5. That

<meta>catchword: 5. That</meta>

<summary>This page explains the "eternal generation" of the Trinity, defining the Father as the Will, the Son as the Mind or Heart, and the Holy Spirit as the "egress" or outward flow of divine power.</summary>
<keywords>Trinity, Father, Son, Holy Spirit, divine will, divine mind, eternal generation, Jakob Böhme, theosophy, emanation</keywords>

5. That which has flown forth from the one eternal will is called the *lubet* <term>original: "lubet"; from the Latin *libitum*, meaning a delight, pleasure, or divine inclination.</term> of the Deity, or the eternal Wisdom. This is the eternal origin of all powers, colors, and virtues. Through this, the threefold spirit in this *lubet* comes to a desiring of these powers, colors, and virtues. This desiring is an "impressing"—a conceiving of itself. The will conceives the wisdom within the mind; and that which is conceived in the understanding is the eternal Word, consisting of all colors, powers, and virtues, which the eternal will speaks forth by the spirit from the understanding of the mind.

6. And this speaking comes from the eternal seeing, where every power, color, and virtue knows the others distinctly. They all exist in equal property, proportion, or analogy, devoid of weight, limit, or measure; they are also undivided from one another. All the powers, colors, and virtues lie as one, and with the pronouncing or speaking, they unfold themselves and bring themselves into sight.

7. This now is the eternal eye of the abyss <term>original: "abyss"; here referring to the *Ungrund*, the "bottomless" or "groundless" nature of God before manifestation.</term>, in which everything that eternity and time possess is contained. It is called counsel, power, wonder, and virtue. Its

# Of God's first Manifestation

<page-num>16</page-num>

...unique and proper name is Jehovah: who exists beyond all nature and without any beginning of essence. He is a work within himself, generating, finding, or perceiving himself without any kind of origin from or through anything else. He has neither beginning nor end; he is infinite. No number can express his extent and greatness; he is deeper than any thought can reach. He is never far from anything; he is through all and in all. His emergence is everywhere, and apart from him, there is nothing else. He is time and eternity, the ground and the abyss <note>original: "byss and abyss"; Byss (the ground or foundation) and Abyss (the bottomless or groundless) represent the totality of existence and non-existence in Boehme's philosophy.</note>; and yet nothing understands him except for the true understanding, which is God himself.

8. Behold, the holy Trinity has an eternal will within itself! This will is the desiring, and that desiring is the eternal essences which go forth from the heart and the mouth of God through the Holy Spirit. The will that has gone forth from the spirit is the divine power <term>original: "virtue"; here meaning inherent power or efficacy.</term> which encompasses and holds the will, so that within it—just as in God himself—all essences exist. Thus, the blossom of light springs up within it from the heart of God, and that blossom is the...

9. The Son—namely, his heart—is the sun which places all the constellations within a light and pleasant habitation. The Holy Spirit is the air of life, without which neither sun nor constellation would exist. Finally, the solidified spirit of the greater world <note>original: *spiritus majoris mundi*</note> is the chaste virgin before God. <header>Of the many forms of generation</header>

### CHAPTER 3.
#### Concerning the endless and countless ways that pure eternal nature generates itself, both before and after the fall of Lucifer.

1. Reader, understand and consider my writings correctly. We have no power or ability to speak of the "birth" of the Deity, for God never had any beginning but has existed from all eternity. However, we do have the power to speak of God our Father—who he is, how the eternal generation <note>original: "geniture"; the process by which something is produced or born</note> works, and the origin, birth, and activity of nature.

2. It is not necessarily pleasant for us to know the harsh <note>original: "austere"</note>, serious, powerful, fierce, severe, and original birth of nature, as it became separated and was first revealed through the rebellion <note>original: "apostasy"</note> of Lucifer. Our first parents brought the knowledge, feeling, and understanding of this harsh state upon themselves and upon us, their descendants <note>original: "posterity"</note>, through the poisonous venom and infection they received by the provocation and deceit of the devil. Yet, we have a very great need for this knowledge, so that we may thereby learn to recognize the devil, who lives...

<summary>Boehme distinguishes between the eternal, beginningless nature of the Deity and the "birth" of nature, which can be understood by humans. He argues that understanding the harsh, original state of nature—corrupted by Lucifer's rebellion—is essential for recognizing the devil's influence on humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, creation, Lucifer, Fall of Man, eternal nature, Deity, generation, original sin, apostasy</keywords>

--- Page 325 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion regarding the "harsh birth" of nature and the influence of the devil. It concludes the sentence describing where the devil dwells and moves into an explanation of the purpose of Boehme's work: the restoration of humanity's original, holy state before the Fall.</meta>

<page-num>19</page-num>
<header>Of pure eternal Nature.</header>

...dwells in the strongest, most severe, and cruel birth of all. We must also learn to know our own enemy, **SELF**, which our first parents awakened and roused up, and which we carry within us—indeed, which we ourselves now are. 

My writing is solely for this purpose: so that man may learn to know what he is and what he was in the beginning. Originally, he was a very glorious, eternal, and holy man who would never have known the gate of the strong and harsh <note>original: "austere"</note> birth in eternity if he had not allowed himself to lust after it through the temptation and infection of the devil, and if he had not eaten of that forbidden fruit. By this act, he became such a naked and vain man, in a bestial <note>original: "beastial"</note> form (as we now find ourselves to be), and lost the heavenly garment [or body] of divine power. Now we live in the kingdom of the devil, in the poisonous essence <note>original: "virtue"</note> of the infected saltpeter <term>sal-nitre; in Boehme’s thought, this refers to a volatile, foundational force in nature</term>, and feed upon infected food. 

It is necessary for us to learn to know ourselves—what we are, and how we may be redeemed from this anguished and harsh birth, so that we may be regenerated or born anew. We must live as the "new man" (who is like the first man before the Fall) in Christ, our regenerator.

<sig>M 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: 3. For</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that his goal is to help humanity recognize their fallen, "bestial" state and their original holy nature. He emphasizes that spiritual rebirth through Christ is the only way to escape the harsh, corrupted nature inherited from the Fall.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, the Fall, original sin, self-will, sal-nitre, regeneration, Christ, the new man, human nature</keywords>

--- Page 326 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's argument regarding the necessity of deep spiritual knowledge. He asserts that merely knowing the "history" of the Fall and salvation is insufficient; one must understand the underlying mechanics of how humanity fell and how the new birth occurs within the soul.</meta>

<page-num>20</page-num>
<header>*Of the manifold engendering*</header>

3. Even if I were to speak or write endlessly about the Fall and the regeneration <note>original: "regeneration"; the process of being spiritually reborn</note> in Christ, yet did not reach the root and foundation—explaining what the Fall actually was, how it caused us to perish, what that quality <note>original: "property"; in Boehme’s thought, this refers to a specific spiritual characteristic or force</note> is which God hates, and how it was brought about contrary to God’s command and will—what would I actually understand? Absolutely nothing! Furthermore, how could I shun or avoid something I have no knowledge of? Or how could I strive to achieve the new birth and surrender myself to it if I do not know how, in what way, or by what means to do so?

4. It is quite true that the world is full of books about the Fall and the new birth. However, in most theological books, there is nothing but the historical account and the statement that we should be regenerated in Christ. But what do I learn from this? Nothing, except the history: that such a thing has happened in the past, has happened again since, and ought to be done.

5. If you are born of God, reach toward understanding what God and the eternal birth are; for you will see, feel, and find that all of creation must have a higher root from which it emerged—a root that is not visible, but hidden. 25. My dear reader, if you are able, answer this question. Look into the deep, the stars, the elements, and into all living creatures—plants, trees, metals, and stones—as well as heaven and earth, and you will not find it. Now you will say, "Where then shall I find it?" Indeed, I cannot even lend you the key that will lead you to it. But I will direct you to where you *shall* find it; it lies in the third chapter of the Gospel of John, in these words:

> "You must be born again <note>original: "anew"</note> of water and of the Holy Spirit."

This Spirit is the key; when you attain it and receive it, go before the first principle <term>A "principle" in this context refers to a fundamental source, a primary law, or an original state of being from which other things proceed.</term> and open the first root from which this world and all visible and perceptible <note>original: "sensible"</note> things originated.

26. But you will say, "This is only God, and he is a spirit, and has created all things out of nothing." It is very true: he is a spirit, and in our sight he is as nothing <note>The author refers to the "Ungrund" or the "Nothing," a mystical concept where God exists as an undifferentiated, infinite potential before creation.</note>. If we did not have some knowledge of him through the creation, we would know nothing of him at all; and if he had not existed from all eternity, nothing could ever have been.

<page-num>35</page-num>

# The Fall of *Lucifer*

27. But what do you think existed before the time of this world’s creation? For out of that preceding state came the root of this earth and its stones, as well as the stars and the elements. But what is this root made of? You will find nothing in it but bitterness, harshness, astringent sourness <term>In Boehme’s philosophy, these qualities represent the "First Principle" or the harsh, contractive forces of nature that exist before they are enlightened by God’s light.</term>, and fire. These are all one thing: the pure eternal element from which all outward, natural things were produced <note>original: "generated"</note> after the fall of *Lucifer*. For before his fall, there was only one pure element. Now, you cannot find God within these harsh forms; the pure Deity is incomprehensible, unperceivable, almighty, and all-powerful. Where, then, can people find God?

28. Here, open your noble mind and search further. Since God is only good, where does evil come from? And since he alone is the life, the light, and the holy power—as is undeniably true—where does the anger of God come from? Where does the devil and his evil will come from? And what is the origin <note>original: "original"</note> of hellfire? Considering there was nothing before God manifested himself in creation except God alone, who was and is a spirit, and remains so for eternity. From...

...from where, then, is the primary substance <note>original: "first matter"</note> of evil? Here, blind reason <term>In Boehme’s writing, "blind reason" refers to human logic that lacks spiritual revelation and therefore reaches false conclusions about God.</term> judges that there must have been a will within the Spirit of God to generate the source and fountain of anger and evil.

29. But the Scripture says the devil was created as a holy angel; and it further says, "You are not a God who wills evil" <note>Psalm 5:4</note>. And through Ezekiel, God declares that as surely as he lives, he does not desire the death of a sinner <note>Ezekiel 33:11</note>. This is proven by God’s earnest and severe punishment of the devil and of all sinners, showing that he takes no pleasure in death.

30. What, then, is the primary material <note>original: "first matter"</note> of evil within the devil? And what moved him to anger, considering he was created out of the original, eternal Spirit of God? Or what is the origin of evil and of hell, where the devils shall remain forever after this world—with its stars, elements, earth, and stones—perishes at the end of time?

31. which he himself formed; and because the light of God is his eternal shame, he is therefore God's enemy, because he no longer exists within the light of God.

32. Now, human reason cannot argue that God ever used any material to create evil; if that were true, the devil could justify himself by saying he was made evil and created from evil matter. Instead, God created him out of nothing but his own divine essence, just as he did with the other angels. As it is written: "Through him, and in him, are all things" <note>Romans 11:36</note>. His alone is the kingdom, the power, and the glory; and all things exist within him, as the Holy Scriptures witness. If this were not the case, no sin could be attributed to the devil or to humanity, for they would not be eternal if they did not have their origin within God himself.

33. In contrast, no sin is attributed to an animal <note>original: "beast"</note>, which is created out of physical matter and has its origin in the third principle <term>In Boehme’s philosophy, the "Third Principle" is the visible, material world governed by the four elements and the stars.</term> of this outward world, within the elementary and sidereal <note>original: "syderial," meaning related to the stars or astral influences</note> kingdom. An animal's spirit does not reach the first principle <term>The "First Principle" refers to the eternal, foundational source of nature—the "fire" or power from which all things emerge.</term>. Animals exist within a state of decay <note>original: "corruptibility"</note> and do not reach the level of the Deity as the devil and the human soul do.

# Regarding Eternal Nature (continued)

34. 35. God could have helped man in some other way if man had been created from any other substance <note>original: "matter"</note> besides God's own essence.

36. And what do you think our blessed Lord brought to humanity, into the flesh, when he came? He brought nothing else but what Adam and our mother Eve lost in Paradise! 37. If, therefore, you wish to speak or think of God, you must consider that he is All; and you must search further into the **three principles** <term>In Boehme’s theology, the "three principles" are the three modes of divine manifestation: the "First Principle" (dark fire/power), the "Second Principle" (light/love), and the "Third Principle" (the outward world/nature).</term>, in which you will find what God is. You will find what the wrath, the devil, sin, and hell are; also what the angels, humanity, and beasts are, and how the separation from the **one pure element** <term>Boehme refers here to the "Holy Element" or spiritual substance of heaven, which he believed fractured into the four physical elements after the Fall.</term> and the subsequent variation occurred. You will find the source from which all things have proceeded, and likewise the creation of this world.

38. Only, dear Reader, I will sincerely warn <note>original: "admonish"</note> you: if you are not currently like the **prodigal or lost son** <note>Refers to the biblical parable in Luke 15, symbolizing a sinner returning to God with humility.</note> returning to his father again, you had better leave my books alone; they will do you harm. For Satan, the great prince, will not hesitate to deceive you, because he stands exposed <note>original: "naked"</note> in these books before the children of God, and is put to open shame before them for his misdeeds. Therefore, if you are in his service, and still love and favor the soft, delicate, animalistic <note>original: "beastial"</note> flesh, do not read my books—they will do you harm. But if you will not take warning, and some misfortune befalls you, I will be guiltless; blame nobody but yourself. For I write down what I know at this present time as a record <note>original: "memorial"</note>...

39. Since we can find nothing in all of nature about which we may say, "This is **God**," or "Here is **God**"—from which we might conclude **God** to be some strange thing that our imaginations portray <note>original: "fancies figure"</note> to us—and since he himself testifies that the kingdom and the power are his, from eternity to eternity, and he calls himself **Father** [and the **Son**, the **second Person** in the **Trinity**, begotten of his **Father**], we must therefore seek him in the origin <note>original: "original"</note> of his manifesting himself in the **triune One**: namely, **Father, Son, and Spirit**, from whom all creation proceeded. We can say nothing else but that the first principle in creation is **God the Father** himself, as the source or fountain of life.

40. Yet, in the origin of life, there is found a most fierce and strong birth—namely, harshness, bitterness, anguish, and fire. 41. All the angels [of whom Lucifer and his hosts were part], as well as the soul of man, come out of this same spirit. The angels were created first and remained within that spirit at the time they were made physical <note>original: "corporeal"</note>, [though they were also endowed with the light of the second principle]. The soul of man is breathed in from the Spirit of God at the time of the creation of the body within the one pure element, which is the root of the third principle [or this outward, physical world]. 47. Although here the human tongue cannot utter, declare, express, or fathom this great depth—where there is neither number nor end—yet we have the power to speak of it, just as children talk of their father.

48. Now, since we are to speak of the Holy Trinity, we must first say that there is one God. He is called God the Father and Creator of all things, who is almighty and all-in-all. All things belong to him; all things proceed from and within him, and in him they remain eternally. We also say that he is three in person, and has from eternity generated his Son out of himself. The Son is his heart, light, and love; yet they are not two, but one eternal essence. Furthermore, we say—and Scripture tells us—that there is a Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> who proceeds from the Father and the Son, and there is but one essence in the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.

# Of Eternal Nature

49. For behold, the Father is the original essence of all essences. We can perceive that the birth of the Son takes its origin in the fire and achieves his personality and name in the kindling of the soft, white, and clear light, which is he himself. This produces the "second principle," <note>In Boehme's cosmology, the "second principle" refers to the world of light and love, distinct from the "first principle" of dark fire/power.</note> and his own essence is the power and the light; therefore, he is rightly called the power or virtue <note>original: "virtue"</note> of the Father.

50. But the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> is not known or revealed in the origin of the Father before the light—or the Son—breaks forth. But when the gentle fountain springs up in the light, then he goes out as a strong, almighty spirit in great joy from the pleasant source of water and of the light. He creates the forms [shaping and figuring] and images [or species], and he is the center in all created essences; in which center the light of life takes its origin, within the light of the Son or the heart of the Father. And the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> is a distinct <note>original: "several"</note> person because he proceeds [as a living power and virtue] from the Father and the Son, and he confirms the birth, generation, or working of the Holy Trinity.

51. Thus you may easily find that the manifestation of the pure Deity in the birth or begetting of the holy Trinity stands in the source of water <note>Boehme uses "water" here to describe a spiritual substance or "divine corporality"—the cooling, nourishing essence that balances the fire of the Father.</note>, in the light of the *Son*; and moreover that the powerful spirit is within it, the former, framer, and fashioner of all things. Thus, from the heaven in this forming or framing comes the eternal pure holy element; and the framing and generating out of it, *to infinity* <note>original: "in infinitum"</note>, is the *Paradise* of *God*. As the highly worthy *Moses* writes, “the *Spirit* of *God* moved upon the water,” [of the dark chaos, after the fall of *Lucifer*] in the framing, forming, or fashioning of the third principle, namely, this world.

53. Thus God is one single, undivided essence, and yet threefold in personal distinction: one God, one Will, one heart, one desire, one pleasure, one beauty, one almightiness, and one fullness of all things, having neither beginning nor end. For if I were to seek the beginning or end of a small dot, or *<term>point</term>* <note>original: punctum</note>, or of a perfect circle, I would be overwhelmed with confusion.

54. And although I have written here about the emergence of the second principle <note>In Boehme’s cosmology, the "second principle" refers to the divine world of light and the Son, which balances the "first principle" of dark, fiery power.</note> and the birth of the divine essence in the Trinity as if it had a beginning, you must not understand it as having any start. The eternal manifestation of the pure Deity is just so: without beginning or end, even in its original state in creation. I am permitted to write about these origins so that mankind might learn to know themselves—to understand what they are, and what God in the Triune One, heaven, angels, demons, and hell are. This also reveals what the wrath of God and hellfire are: the result of the extinguishing of the divine light.

But because they were to live eternally, they had to be formed out of the first principle, which is an unbreakable bond <note>original: "indissoluble band"</note>. They were meant to look upon the Heart or Son of God to receive His light, and to feed upon the Word; this food was to be their holy

*The Creation of Angels, and of Lucifer,*

preservation, and to keep their image clear and bright. In the same way, the Heart or Son of God in the second principle <note>original: "second principle"; in Boehme’s thought, this is the realm of light, love, and divine manifestation.</note> reveals and enlightens the Father—that is, the first principle <note>original: "first principle"; the eternal, fiery foundation or "wrath" of God's power that requires the light of the Son to become Paradise.</note>. Within these two principles, divine power, the pure element of paradise, and the kingdom of heaven spring forth.

2. This is the state of those angels who remained in the kingdom of heaven within the first paradise. They exist within the first principle in an unbreakable bond <note>original: "indissoluble band"; a connection to the eternal foundation of existence that cannot be broken.</note>, yet they are enlightened by the Son in the second principle. Their food is the Divine Word, and their thoughts and minds are centered on the will of the Trinity within the Godhead. ## CHAPTER 5.

->Concerning the third principle, or the creation of the material world, with the stars and elements; wherein the first and second principles are more clearly understood.<-

1. The eternal and unbreakable bond, which is the first principle <note>In the author's mystical system, the "first principle" is the foundational, often dark or fiery, essence of God's power.</note> in which the essence of all essences exists, is not easily or quickly understood; therefore, it is necessary that the eager reader should more earnestly consider within himself what he is, and from where his reason, his inner senses, and his thoughts proceed: for in those things, he finds the likeness <note>original: "similitude"</note> of God, especially if he considers and meditates on what his soul is—which is an eternal, incorruptible spirit.

2. For if the reader is born of God in true resignation <term>a mystical state of surrendering one's self-will to God's will; in German, *Gelassenheit*</term>, there is no more direct way for him to come to the knowledge of the third principle <note>The "third principle" refers to the physical, visible universe and the natural world.</note> than by considering the new

# Creation of the Material World

Wisdom has worked in the eternal, inanimate <term>genetrix</term> <note>Latin: "mother" or "she who brings forth." In Böhme's thought, this is the creative power that gives birth to existence.</note>. This source—in its own nature and until the original light is revealed within it—is without perception. Through this same operation, the dark chaos arose when the light of God departed from Lucifer due to his self-exaltation. As a result, the fierce source of fire was ignited, and the creation of the **third principle** <note>The physical, material world.</note> followed; Lucifer was then shut up within it like a prisoner. We should understand, however, that he originally had his royal seat in the region of this world. This is why Christ calls him a "prince of this world," <note>John 12:31</note> though his rule is now restricted to the kingdom of darkness, in the realm of the **first principle** <note>The dark, fiery foundation of existence that lacks the light of God's love.</note> of this world.

7. When God moved Himself to create, it was within the **first principle**, with its dark, harsh, bitter, and fiery properties. However, these were entirely hidden by the light, or the Son of God, who had preserved them from eternity and kept them pleasant, clear, and bright. When He created the angels, He created them out of the indissoluble bond in Paradise, within the holy <term>matrix</term> <note>Latin: "womb."</note> of light. This matrix is nothing other than the **first** and **second principles** united, and the angels were given bodies from the moving Spirit of God.

# CHAPTER VI.

## Regarding the separation in the creation of this world, or the third principle.

1. If we consider the specific differences, forms, and variations through which everything has its own peculiar essence in the third principle <note>Boehme defines the "Third Principle" as the visible, material world, which is a manifestation of the underlying spiritual realm.</note> of this world, we see how the starry heavens sprang up. In this context, it refers to the sun's inherent power or life-giving efficacy.</note>, the stars could neither act nor accomplish anything. 3. Indeed, we see that all things spring out of the eternal mother. But if we wish to see the foundation of this, we must look upon the "first mother." In her original state, without the light, she is sour, harsh, dark, hard, and cold; yet the "spirit of water" exists within her for the production of light, and within the power <note>original: "virtue"</note> of that light lies the pure element of eternal nature.

4. In her expansion and multiplication, she has generated this world and every creature within it, reflecting her own birth. <header>This World in the Third Principle</header>

[This world] has become material and has the external sun for its light and life. *However, that light and life is not the wise master <note>The "wise master" refers to the Creator or the divine architect of the universe.</note> himself; rather, the wise master [who is God] keeps that light and life stable and continuing in the external matrix <note>original: "matrix"; the womb or formative medium of the physical world</note> of this world to manifest eternal wisdom.* For the sun is neither the power <note>original: "virtue"</note> nor the wisdom of God, but had a beginning through the will of God and will eventually return into its ether. It continually works according to its own nature, giving life and killing, without regard for whether something is evil, crooked, lame, or good, beautiful or powerful. It causes life and death, provides power and strength, and destroys them again; and all of this happens without any conscious wisdom of its own. By this, we may perceive that the sun is not divine providence or wisdom itself, as the heathens <note>original: "heathens"; referring here to ancient peoples who worshiped the sun as a deity</note> supposed, foolishly relying upon its power.

5. It seems strange and wonderful to "fine reason" <note>original: "FINE REASON"; Boehme uses this term to describe intellectual logic that lacks spiritual or divine insight</note> to consider how God has created the sun, the stars, and the four elements. This is especially true when reason contemplates and considers the earth with its hard stones and very rough, unrefined <note>original: "indigested"</note>, harsh substance, and sees that there are great rocks and cliffs created,

16. Before the fall of Lucifer and the creation of this world, everything existed within everything else in the eternal generation—within the pure eternal element. This was like a powerful, wrestling "love-play," without any material substance, solidification <note>original: "coagulation"</note>, or individual creatures.

17. But when Lucifer rebelled <note>original: "apostacy"</note>, all the properties of nature were ignited by the movement of the Divine Word. Through the fire of the *salnitral flagrat* <note>original: "salnitral flagrat." As noted previously, Boehme uses this to describe a divine, explosive flash of energy that initiates manifestation.</note>, every property divided itself. They were then gathered together by the awakened "astringent impression"—the first form of nature, which is called the *Fiat* <note>original: "Fiat." Latin: "Let it be done." This refers to the creative command of God in Genesis.</note>. In this way, each property solidified according to its nature: the refined and pure according to its kind, and the coarse <note>original: "gross"</note> according to its kind. This happened across many levels, just as the eternal generation of nature flows from a single Unity into infinite variety <note>original: "multiplicity"</note>.

18. Good and evil are revealed in the stars <term>astrum</term> or constellation; for the wrathful, fiery power of eternal nature is present there, as is...

There it still stands open for humanity, if anyone will depart from the essence of this world and enter into it through the path that Christ has opened.

# Of the Separation in the Creation, etc.

37. It is written by Moses that "God looked upon all that he had made, and behold, it was very good." Now humanity <note>original: "man"</note>, for whose sake the earth was cursed, had not yet introduced anything into the earth that made it so evil as to cause God to curse it—except for the false and faithless imagination of his desire to eat of the knowledge of good and evil. This desire was intended to awaken the vanity of nature <note>The "vanity of nature" refers to the unstable, fleeting, and conflicted state of the physical world after the fall.</note> within him, so that he might know both evil and good. 

From that desire, hunger entered into the earth, from which the outward body was extracted as a mass <note>original: "masse"</note>. This body then directed the hunger of its desire back toward its mother [the earth] and raised up the root of vanity out of the dark impression of the center of nature. From this, the tree of temptation—namely, the knowledge of evil and good—became manifest to him; and when he ate from it, the earth was cursed for his sake.

<summary>The author explains that the earth was originally "very good" and was only cursed because man’s internal desire for the knowledge of good and evil projected a "hunger" back into the earth, manifesting the tree of temptation.</summary>

## Chapter VII

### Concerning the Mystery of Creation

1. We are now to consider the greatest mystery of this outward world, which exists between the elements and the *astrum* <note>original: "astrum"; the author uses this term to refer to the "starry" or celestial influence that governs the inner life and form of physical things.</note>. The elemental spirit is distinct from the astral spirit, and yet they are not separated from one another. They dwell within each other like body and soul, but the one is not the other. The astral spirit forms its own bodies just as the elemental spirit does, and this occurs in all creatures—including both animals and plants <note>original: "vegetables"</note>.

2. All things in this world have a twofold body: namely, an elemental body made from fire, air, water, and earth; and a spiritual body derived from the *astrum*. Likewise, they have a twofold spirit: one astral and the other elemental.

3. Humanity alone, among all earthly creatures, has a threefold body and spirit. For humans also have the internal spiritual world within them, which is likewise twofold—namely, light and darkness—and also corporeal... <note>The text ends mid-word at "corpo-", likely intending "corporeal."</note>

<summary>This page explains that all created things have a dual nature consisting of elemental (physical) and astral (celestial) spirits and bodies. It distinguishes humanity as unique for possessing a threefold nature that incorporates the internal spiritual realms of light and darkness.</summary>

<keywords>creation, astrum, elemental spirit, astral spirit, threefold body, duality of nature, human composition, spiritual world</keywords>
