->Of the Mystery of the Creation.<-

...corporeally and spiritually. This internal spirit is the soul, but the internal body comes from the water of the holy element. This body "died" in Adam—meaning it vanished from his life—when the divine power departed from him and refused to dwell within his awakened vanity <note>original: "vanity"; in this context, it refers to the prideful, empty, or fallen state of earthly existence that Adam entered after the Fall.</note>.

4. This holy body must be regenerated if a person's spirit is to see God; otherwise, he cannot see Him. As our Lord told Nicodemus:
> "Unless a man is born again of water and of the Spirit, he cannot see, nor enter into the kingdom of God" <note>John 3:3-5</note>.
He must be born anew of the water of the holy, pure element in the Spirit of God, who has revealed Himself in Christ [within us] through this same water-source <note>The author often uses the concept of a "fountain" or "source" to describe the flowing nature of divine properties.</note>. This is so that his vanished holy body may be brought to life in the holy water and spirit; otherwise, the soul has no perception or vision in the holy life of God.

5. This twofold outward body—namely, the astral <note>original: "astral"; relating to the "stars" or celestial influences that Böhme believed governed the mind and form.</note> and the elemental—must now be carefully pondered and precisely considered if we wish to understand nature. For within these bodies, the governing power of all external creatures and essences is contained. These two are often in conflict with one another, which is the source of sickness,

7. The elemental spirit and body are inanimate and lack understanding; they possess only lust and desire. Their true life is like that of vegetation <note>The author suggests that the elemental body functions purely on a biological or instinctive level, much like a plant, without inherent reason.</note>. For the air (or breath) has no understanding without the astral influence <note>original: "astrum"; referring to the celestial or cosmic forces that Böhme believed governed the human mind and the physical world.</note>. This astral influence provides the distinct understanding of all essences within the elements. The sidereal body dwells within the elemental body just as the world of light dwells within the darkness. It is the sidereal spirit, originating from the astral principle, that provides reason and is the true rational life; however, it has no true divine perception or conception of spiritual things. It is the inward light, and the power of that light, which provides a person with true divine understanding.

## CHAPTER VIII

### On the difference between the heavenly and the earthly man.

1. The text in Moses speaks very clearly and fully where it says, 
> “And God created man in his own image; 
> “in the image of God he created him: 
> “male and female he created them.” 
By the "creating" is understood the body, which is twofold: namely, a spiritual body and a corporeal body [that is, a fleshly body]. The <term>word "let there be"</term> <note>original: "verbum fiat"; the divine command of creation from Genesis.</note> itself shaped <note>original: "figurize"</note> and formed it into a spiritual man out of all the three principles: namely, according to the inward divine world, according to the fiery light world, and the outward world.

## Heavenly and Earthly Man.

> “...thing.” <note>Continuing from the previous page: "The flesh profits nothing" (John 6:63).</note> John 6. 11. Everything on man that is earthly is beastly and corruptible, and is not the true man. For although God created an external body for man out of the *slime* <note>original: "limus"; Latin for mud or dust, referring to Genesis 2:7.</note> of the earth, we should not think of it as it is now. For the true human body, according to the inward world, is a spiritual <term>sulfur</term>, a spiritual <term>mercury</term>, and a spiritual <term>salt</term> <note>In the alchemy and philosophy of Jacob Boehme, these three principles (tria prima) represent the fundamental qualities of all matter: energy/desire (sulfur), movement/spirit (mercury), and substance/corporeality (salt).</note>. Every property of the soul has a bodily or essential quality corresponding to it.

12. God created such a body as the soul was in its essence—namely, in the breathed word of the understanding—and breathed the soul into it for the sake of understanding. The true, real body, which is hidden within the coarseness, is a spiritual body compared to that coarseness. It was indeed created in flesh and blood, but in a stable, steadfast, and incorruptible flesh and blood.

13. Through the lust and imagination of Adam, the coarseness was made manifest. The true outward body is a sulfurous, <term>mercurial</term> <note>original: "mercurial"; referring to the principle of motion, communication, and life-force.</note>—

the tree of knowledge of good and evil in the middle.

2. Here a veil lies before the face of Moses; because he had such a bright, shining face, sinful Israel could not look him in the eye <note>original: "look him in the face"; referring to Exodus 34:29-35, where Moses' face shone after speaking with God, requiring him to wear a veil.</note>. Likewise, the "man of vanity" <term>man of vanity</term> <note>original: "man of vanity"; referring to the prideful, earthly person who is focused only on physical reality and temporary things.</note> is not worthy to know what Paradise is. Although it is granted to us to know it through the inward, hidden man, yet by this description, we will seem silent and incomprehensible to the beast-like nature <note>original: "dumb to the beast"; referring to the carnal or animalistic mind of man which cannot grasp spiritual truths.</note>. However, we will be sufficiently understood by our fellow students in the school of the Great Master.

3. Poor reason—which departed with Adam out of Paradise—asks: where is Paradise to be found? Is it far off, or near? Or, when souls go into Paradise, where do they go? Is it within the space of this world, or outside of this world above the stars? Where is it that God dwells with the angels? And where is that desirable homeland where there is no death? Since there is no sun or stars in it, reason concludes it cannot be in this world; otherwise, it would have been found long ago.

4. Beloved reason: one cannot lend a key to another to unlock this with; and

If anyone has a key, he cannot open the way for another. For example, the Antichrist boasts that he has the keys of heaven and hell; and while it is true that a person may possess the keys to both in this lifetime, he cannot open them for anyone else. Every person must unlock it with their own key, or else they cannot enter; for the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> is the key, and when someone has that key, they may go both in and out.

5. Paradise was the heavenly essentiality <term>essentiality</term> <note>original: "essentiality"; referring here to the spiritual substance or the inner, divine reality that gives form to the physical.</note> of the second principle <term>second principle</term> <note>In Jacob Boehme’s complex theology, the "second principle" represents the realm of Light, Divine Love, and the Son, which stands in contrast to the "first principle" of God's fire or wrath.</note>. It blossomed in the beginning of the world through the earthly essence, just as eternity exists within time and divine power moves through all things—and yet, it is neither grasped nor understood by any earthly thing in its own self-hood <term>self-hood</term> <note>original: "self-hood"; the state of being focused on one's own ego or earthly existence, separate from the divine spirit.</note>.

6. In Paradise, the essence of the divine world permeated the essence of time, just as the sun permeates the fruit upon a tree and works effectively within it to bring it to a state of delight, so that it is lovely to look upon and good to eat. We are to understand the Garden of Eden in the same way.

7. The Garden of Eden was a physical place on the earth where man was tempted, and Paradise was in heaven; and yet, Paradise was also within the Garden of Eden. For just as Adam, before his sleep,

The devil holds power, honor, pleasure, and worldly joy in his hand; but the root of these things is death and the fire of hell. After the end of time, nothing will remain except light and darkness. In these, the source or property will remain in each as it has been from eternity, and one shall not encompass the other.

13. Whether God will create more after this world’s time, my spirit does not know. It understands no further than what is in its center where it lives, and where Paradise and the kingdom of heaven reside <note>original: "stands"</note>.

### CHAPTER X.

#### Of the paradisiacal dominion, life, and condition, showing how it would have been if Adam had not fallen.

1. I know the sophist <note>original: "Sophist"; a term used here for a critic who relies on worldly logic or clever but misleading reasoning rather than spiritual insight.</note> will criticize me here and dismiss this as something impossible for me to know, since I was not there to see it myself. To him I say: *I, in the essence of my soul and body—back when I was not yet "I," but part of Adam's essence—was there and did myself foolishly throw away my glory in Adam. But since Christ has restored it to me again, I see in the Spirit of Christ what I was in Paradise, what I am now in sin, and what I shall be again.* Therefore, let no one cry out that this is unknowable. For although I indeed do not know it on my own, the Spirit of Christ knows it within me, and I shall write from that knowledge.

The inward essence loved the outward as its manifestation, and the outward loved the inward

<summary>This page explains Adam’s original composition as a harmonious union of a heavenly spiritual body and a refined physical body. These two essences existed in a state of mutual love and divine purity before being corrupted by the Fall.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the description of Adam’s original state, specifically the harmonious union of his spiritual and physical essences and how he would have reproduced without the Fall.</meta>

<page-num>108</page-num>

<header>As Adam should have been</header>

as its greatest sweetness, joyfulness, and precious pearl: as its most beloved spouse or consort. And yet they were not two, but only one body of a two-fold essence: namely, one inward, heavenly, and holy essence; and one from the essence of time. These were joined and betrothed to each other for an eternal existence.

9. And the magical impregnation, conception, and birth consisted in this love-desire. For the <term>tincture</term> <note>tincture: the essential spiritual quality or "life-force" that infuses a being with fire and light</note> penetrated through both essences—the inward and the outward—and stirred up the desire. Within the desire was the <term>Fiat</term> <note>original: "Fiat"; Latin for "let it be done," referring here to the creative command or power of God</note>, which the love-<term>lubet</term> <note>lubet: from the German *Lust* or Latin *libitum*, meaning "delight" or "inclination"; the divine imagination or joyful will</note> [or imagination] conceived and brought into a substance. Thus, the *likeness* of the *express image* was formed in this substance, being a *spiritual image* according to the first one God created. For just as the *Fiat* conceived and formed the first image—namely *Adam*—the likeness was also conceived out of the first for propagation <note>propagation: the process of breeding or reproducing</note>.

10. Understand that if *Adam* had remained steadfast <note>original: "had stood"</note>, then the magical birth would have been carried out this way—not by a separate, physical discharge from *Adam’s* body as it happens now, but just as the sun shines through water and does not tear or damage it.

<summary>This page describes how Adam’s original dual essence (heavenly and temporal) would have allowed for a "magical birth." This spiritual form of reproduction would have occurred through divine desire and the creative power of the Fiat, rather than through physical labor or injury to the body.</summary>

<keywords>Adam, Fiat, essence, impregnation, tincture, lubet, spiritual image, express image, magical birth, reproduction, Jacob Böhme</keywords>

--- Page 417 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how Adam would have reproduced in his original, unfallen state—a process described as a painless, spiritual "bringing forth" rather than physical birth.</meta>

<page-num>109</page-num>

# If he had not fallen.

Even so, the spiritual body—namely, the child—would have been brought forth. In its coming out, it would have become a physical substance without pain, worry, or distress, but in great joy and delight. It would have been a virgin-like image, completely perfect according to the first [Adam].

11. This birth—after Venus’s womb <note>original: "Venus's matrix"; in this context, referring to the feminine generative principle or the "mother" power of nature</note> was taken from Adam and formed into a woman—had to be done through anguish, trouble, pain <note>original: "smart"</note>, pangs, and distress. As God said to Eve, "I will multiply your sorrows when you conceive; you shall now bring forth children with sorrow, and your will shall be subject to your husband." Why? Because she sprang forth from the man’s will. Eve was half of Adam—specifically, the part in which Adam should have loved himself and embodied his offspring. But when he did not remain steadfast <note>original: "stood not"</note>, that part was taken from him in his sleep and formed into a woman. Therefore, when Adam saw her, he said: "She shall be called woman [or manness <note>manness: a literal translation of the German *Männin*, used to show that "woman" is linguistically and physically derived from "man"</note>] because she is taken out of man."

12. Man should have walked naked upon the earth, for the heavenly inward part permeated the outward, and brightness was

<meta>catchword: his</meta>

<summary>This page describes how the Fall changed reproduction from a joyful, spiritual process into the painful physical birth experienced by women after the separation of Eve from Adam. It emphasizes that Eve was originally the internal part of Adam through which he was meant to love himself and propagate his kind.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Eve, the Fall, Venus's matrix, manness, childbirth, spiritual body, propagation</keywords>

<page-num>110</page-num>

[his] clothing; he existed in great beauty, glory, joy, and delight, with a childlike mind. He was meant to eat and drink in a magical way—not into the body as we do now, but in the mouth. That was where the separation <note>separation: the spiritual process of extracting the essence of food without needing a digestive tract; the author suggests Adam's "digestion" was instantaneous and non-physical</note> occurred, for the fruit of Paradise was consumed in the same way.

***

## CHAPTER 11

### Of the birth of the woman, Adam’s being ordained to the outward life, and his restoration by Jesus Christ.

<margin>1.</margin> Now Moses says that the Lord God said, "It is not good that man should be alone; we will make a helper suited <note>original: "help meet"; meaning a fitting or matching companion</note> for him." When God had created all creatures along with the entire host of living things, Moses says, "And God beheld all things which he had made; and lo, it was very good," and he confirmed that everything should propagate itself. But here he says of man, "It is not good he should be alone." For God saw man's miserable fall—that through his own desires <note>original: "lusts"; referring here to a turning of the will toward the physical world</note>, he could no longer reproduce himself through spiritual means <note>original: "magically propagate"</note>. Therefore, God said, "We will make a helper suited for him."

<summary>This page explains that Adam's original body did not require a physical digestive system and introduces the creation of Eve as a response to Adam's inability to reproduce spiritually after his internal "fall."</summary>

<keywords>Adam, Eve, Paradise, Creation, Moses, magical propagation, helper suited, Fall of Man</keywords>

--- Page 419 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the creation of Eve and the spiritual implications of the "deep sleep" and the rib, linking Adam's side to Christ's side on the cross.</meta>

# Adam Ordained to the Outward Life

2. And Moses says, “God caused a deep sleep to fall upon the man, and he slept; and he took one of the ribs out of his side, and made [or built] a woman from it, and closed up the place with flesh.” Moses says the woman was made of a rib out of Adam’s side; who can understand this without divine light? For here a veil lies before the brightness of Moses’s face, because of the unworthiness of the bestial man <note>original: "beastial man"; referring to humanity in its fallen, animalistic state, which lacks the spiritual perception to understand the deeper mysteries of scripture</note>.

3. For we find that the woman was taken and formed in the <term>Fiat</term> <note>original: "Fiat"; the divine command of creation, from the Latin phrase *fiat lux*, "let there be light"</note> out of Adam’s essence—out of both his body and soul. But the rib signifies Adam’s dissolution or breaking—namely, that this body should and would be destroyed. For in the place of this rib, Longinus’s spear <note>Longinus is the traditional name of the Roman soldier who pierced the side of Jesus with a spear during the Crucifixion (John 19:34).</note> must later enter when Christ was crucified, to tincture <note>original: "tincture"; in the author's mystical theology, this means to infuse or permeate a substance with a spiritual or healing quality</note> and heal the breach in the wrath of God with heavenly blood.

4. Now, when Adam’s hunger was set toward the earthly world, it did—by its magnetic power—press into his beautiful image the vanity of evil and good. Because of this, the heavenly image of the angelic world’s essence disappeared, as if a man should introduce some strange matter into a burning and brightly shining candle, whereby it should become

<meta>catchword: come</meta>

<summary>This page explains the mystical significance of the creation of Eve from Adam’s rib, arguing that the rib represents the eventual "breaking" of the physical body and prefigures the piercing of Christ’s side. It also describes how Adam’s desire for the physical world caused his original heavenly nature to fade.</summary>

<keywords>Adam, Moses, creation of Eve, the rib, Longinus's spear, Christ's crucifixion, Fiat, bestial man, heavenly image, spiritual fall</keywords>

--- Page 420 ---
<meta>This page continues the description of Adam's fall from a divine state into a temporal, physical existence, linking his "deep sleep" to the creation of Eve and the future sacrifice of Christ.</meta>

## Of Adam in his Outward Life

...become dark and was at last wholly extinguished. The same happened to Adam, for by his lust he directed his will and desire away from God and toward self-centeredness <note>original: "self-hood"; in the author's theology, the turning of the will away from God toward the individual self, which is considered the root of sin</note> and vanity. In doing so, he began to obscure the light of his divine life and then broke himself off from God—specifically, from the divine harmony.

5. For even then, he immediately sank down into a swoon, or sleep, which is a symbol of death. For the image of God, which is unchangeable, does not sleep; whatever is eternal exists outside of time. But with this sleep, time was manifested in man; for he slept in the angelic world and woke up to the outward, physical world.

6. His sleep was a true prefiguration <note>original: "type"; a person or event in the Old Testament seen as foreshadowing something in the New Testament</note> of the rest of *Christ* in the grave. There, the new reborn life in *Christ's* humanity had to enter into *Adam's* sleep to awaken it again to eternal life and bring it out of time into eternal existence.

7. And just as *Adam's* essence was broken and divided when the woman was taken out of him, so must *Christ's* body be broken on the cross from the sixth until the ninth hour. For that was the duration of the <term>Fiat</term> <note>original: "Fiat"; the divine command of creation</note> during *Adam's* sleep in the separation of the man and woman [or the creation of the male and female sexes].

<summary>This page explains that Adam's "deep sleep" marked his transition from an eternal, angelic state to a time-bound, physical one. It parallels the creation of Eve (the division of humanity) with the duration of Christ's suffering on the cross, framing Christ's death as the means to awaken humanity from its spiritual slumber.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Christ, self-hood, Fiat, the Fall, deep sleep, crucifixion, regeneration, divine harmony</keywords>

--- Page 421 ---
<meta>This page concludes the discussion of the spiritual parallels between Adam's deep sleep (and the subsequent creation of Eve) and Christ's sacrifice on the cross, emphasizing the restoration of humanity to its original, unified state.</meta>

For in such a span of time, the woman was completely finished and brought forth out of Adam into a female person [or image].

8. And when Christ on the cross had again accomplished this redemption of our virgin-like image from the divided sexes of male and female [from the man and woman], and permeated <note>original: "tinctured"; a term from alchemy and mysticism meaning to imbue or transform a substance with a spiritual quality</note> it with his heavenly blood in divine love, he said, "It is finished." For before this, he stood in Adam’s thirst; that is to say, just as Adam thirsted after vanity <note>original: "vanity"; here referring to the temporary, empty, and illusory things of the physical world</note>, so Christ now filled or satisfied this thirst for vanity with a holy, divine thirst. He turned the soul's will around so that it might once again direct its thirst toward God. And when this was accomplished, he said, "Now it is finished and converted." Christ turned Adam back from vanity during his sleep, moving him away from the division of man and woman and back into the angelic image once more. These mysteries are great and wonderful, though the world cannot understand <note>original: "apprehend"; to grasp mentally or spiritually</note> them; for the world is as blind to these truths as a man born blind is to the sight of this physical world. But whoever considers and finds them shall have great joy in them.

->THE END<- <note>original: "FINIS"; Latin for "the end"</note>

<summary>This page concludes the work by explaining how Christ’s death on the cross reversed Adam's fall, transforming human desire from worldly vanity back toward God and restoring the unified "angelic image."</summary>

### And Restitution by Jesus Christ.

(or in desire <note>original: "lust"; in Boehme’s writing, "lust" refers to a focused longing or will, not necessarily a sexual one</note> at once, as the woman was completely finished and brought forth out of Adam in a pure and angelic image.)

8. And when Christ on the cross had again accomplished the redemption of our virgin-like image—moving it from the divided sexes of male and female back into the true human—and permeated <note>original: "tinctured"; a term from alchemy and mysticism meaning to imbue or transform a substance with a spiritual quality</note> it with his heavenly blood in divine love, he said, "It is finished." For before this, he stood in Adam’s thirst; that is to say, just as Adam thirsted after vanity <note>original: "vanity"; here referring to the temporary, empty, and illusory things of the physical world</note>, so Christ now took this thirst for vanity and filled it with a holy, divine thirst. He turned the soul's will around so that it might once again direct <note>original: "introduce"</note> its thirst toward God. And when this was brought to pass, he said, "Now it is finished and changed." Christ turned Adam back from vanity during his sleep, moving him away from the division of man and woman and back into the angelic image once more. These mysteries are great and wonderful, though the world cannot understand <note>original: "apprehend"; to grasp mentally or spiritually</note> them; for the world is as blind to these truths as a person born blind is to the sight of this physical world. But whoever considers and finds them shall have great joy in them.

->THE END<- <note>original: "FINIS"; Latin for "the end"</note>

<summary>This page concludes the treatise by explaining how Christ’s sacrifice reversed Adam's fall, transforming human desire from worldly vanity back toward God and restoring humanity's original, unified spiritual state.</summary>
